- **First-class genre promotion: boom bap (the six-school genre)** — the engine's biggest roster hole (25 `trap.classic` presets clamped from 135–145 down to a breakbeat tempo) is now a genre: new `src/ai/grooves/boombap.ts` with the full school tree — **golden** (90s NY breakbeat: kick 1 + "and of 3", hard snare 2/4, swung 8ths, 86–96), **jazz** (jazz-rap refinement: lighter kick, ride cymbal, 88–98), **lofi** (Dilla/Madlib off-kilter: late kick, ghost snares, heaviest swing, 78–92), **drumless** (Alchemist school: NO backbeat — pad 4 absent from every pattern, 80–92), **trapbap** (the modern hybrid: boom-bap sample bed + 808 sub at trap tempo, 120–145) and **modern** (Griselda dusty-but-tight: harder snare, cinematic room, 82–94). Full integration: `GENRES`/`Genre` union, `SONG_FORMS` (16-bar verses, no drop labels) + `GENRE_DEFAULT_BPM` 90, parser routing with guards (`boom bap`/`golden era`/`griselda` → boombap; `jazz rap`/`drumless`/`trap bap` school phrases, trap-bap ordered ABOVE the bare `trap` entry; `electro hip hop` stays trap.oldschool and `lofi hip hop` stays ambient), `GENRE_KIT_SWAPS` (the `factory.kick.knock` boom-bap kick + main snare + soft hat), `GENRE_FEEL` (the hardest humanize in the engine — sampled breaks are never grid-locked), warm tone default + character-genre mix treatment, 3 harmony progressions, descriptions, and **23 presets moved off trap.classic** plus 20 new school entries (Premier / Pete Rock / Large Professor / Marley Marl / EPMD; Tribe / De La / Digable / Pharcyde; Dilla / Madlib / Knxwledge / Nujabes; Alchemist / billy woods / Mach-Hommy; Conductor Williams / Griselda depth / Marcberg; trapbap hybrid). Measured reference −9.9 LUFS. New `tests/boombap.test.ts` locks the six-school contract.
- **First-class genre promotion: amapiano (the log-drum genre)** — promoted from `house.amapiano` to a genre with its Wikipedia-documented school tree in new `src/ai/grooves/amapiano.ts`: **yanos** (the core Kabza/MDU sound — quiet four-floor deep-house kick, semiquaver shaker, syncopated rounded log-drum answer on the toms, 110–116), **soulful** (private school piano / Kelvin Momo: sparser log, mellow shaker, jazz-chord room, 108–114), **s'gija** (the stripped gqom-adjacent pocket: harder kick, one hypnotic log phrase, 112–118), **bacardi** (new-age bacardi / Mellow & Sleazy: the slowed Pretoria mutation with raw stab ticks, 108–114), **quantum** (Quantum Sound / RealShaunMusiq: gqom-2.0 taxi kick, 112–120) and **popiano** (the pop-facing variant, Tyla "Water", 108–116). Full integration: `GENRES`/`Genre` union, long-form `SONG_FORMS` (8/16-bar groove sections, "Soulful Break", no drop labels) + `GENRE_DEFAULT_BPM` 112, parser routing (`amapiano`/`yanos`/`afropiano` → amapiano.yanos; `private school piano` → soulful; `s'gija`/`new age bacardi`/`quantum sound`/`popiano` schools; plain `bacardi` guarded to the genre), `GENRE_KIT_SWAPS` (deep soft kick + soft shaker + soft closed hat), `GENRE_FEEL`, warm tone default + character-genre + pump treatment, 3 harmony progressions, descriptions, and the artist lane moved off house (`kabza de small / maphorisa` / `mfr souls` / `daliwonga` / `kelvin momo` / `amapiano wave 2`) plus 8 new school entries (yanos hitmakers/vocal, soulful vocal, s'gija, new-age bacardi, quantum sound, popiano + popiano bridge). The deepest tonal tilt in the library (28.3 dB — the log drum under a quiet floor); measured reference −10.0 LUFS. New `tests/amapiano.test.ts` locks the six-school contract.
- **First-class genre promotion: trance (the breakdown-build-anthem genre)** — promoted from a single `techno.trance` style to a genre with its Wikipedia-documented school tree in new `src/ai/grooves/trance.ts`: **uplifting** (the anthem school: four-floor under the offbeat open-hat mask, supersaw lead, 136–142), **progressive** (Sasha/Digweed smooth end: deeper kick, long phrases, 126–134), **psy** (the Goa lineage: rolling 16th bass tick, 138–148), **tech** (the warehouse crossover: harder kick, metallic percussion, melody pulled back, 134–142), **acid** (the 303 school, 132–142) and **dream** (Robert Miles: soft piano-led euphoria, 128–136). Full integration: `GENRES`/`Genre` union, the defining 120-bar `SONG_FORMS` (16-bar sections; the **Breakdown** strips drums to melody, the **Build** carries the riser, the **Drop** the impact — the template Wikipedia describes as the genre's form) + `GENRE_DEFAULT_BPM` 138, parser routing with guards (`trance`/`uplifting trance`/`vocal trance` → uplifting; `progressive trance`/`tech trance`/`acid trance`/`dream trance`/`psytrance`/`goa` schools; acid/tech trance compounds ordered ABOVE the techno `acid`/`tech` entries so they never fall into techno), `GENRE_KIT_SWAPS` (techno kick + open-hat mask), `GENRE_FEEL` (the most grid-locked in the library), bright tone default + character-genre + pump treatment, 3 harmony progressions, descriptions, and the artist lane moved off techno (`trance`/`paul van dyk` → uplifting, `above and beyond` → progressive, `psytrance`/`astrix`/`infected mushroom` → psy) plus 5 new school entries (uplifting hitmakers, progressive trance, dream trance, tech trance, acid trance). Measured reference −10.2 LUFS (the longest reference form in the library, 120 bars). New `tests/trance.test.ts` locks the school + song-form contract.
- **Disco pop sub-genre** — `house.disco` groove (octave-bass walk, offbeat open hats, snare+clap backbeat, 112–124 swung), parser routing (disco / nu-disco / disko / disco funk → house + style disco, ordered above the funky style so "disco funk" stays disco) and 4 researched artists (Kylie 115–128, Bee Gees 100–110, Chic/Nile Rodgers 115–125, Jessie Ware 108–124).
- **Country pop / afro-pop / latin pop sub-genres** — three dedicated grooves: `house.countrypop` (train-beat boom-chicka, shaker 16ths, 96–126), `house.afropop` (3+3+2 kick cross-rhythm + rim melody, 98–112 — distinct from the afro-house groove) and `house.dembow` (the reggaeton chop: rim-snare on the "and" of 1/3, 88–100). Parser routing (afro pop / afrobeats, reggaeton / latin pop / latinský pop, country pop / nashville pop — bare "country" and "country rap/trap" keep legacy readings) + upgraded lanes (afrobeats wizkid/burna entry → afropop 100–112, latin urban j balvin entry → dembow) + 8 researched artists (Shania 96–122, Kacey 88–118, The Chicks 100–130, Carrie Underwood 92–120; Shakira 92–105, Karol G 88–102, Luis Fonsi 92–100, Rauw Alejandro 90–104). Sub-genre × disco word mixes resolve to the disco groove (deliberate global style order); cross-lane artist blends carry lane A's groove with blended sliders.
- **Electronic sub-genre depth** — seven dedicated grooves: `house.progressive` (soft four-floor + 16th shaker bed, no big backbeat, 124–128), `house.bigbeat` (syncopated breakbeat kick + heavy 2/4 snare, 104–128), `house.moombahton` (four-floor at reggaeton tempo with the dembow chop — the house × dembow combination lane, 105–112), `house.slaphouse` (punch-kick doubles + sparse minimal club, 118–124), `techno.trance` (the offbeat open hat IS the genre, 136–142), `techno.electro` (Detroit machine funk — syncopated 808 kick + tom talk, 126–134) and `trap.deepdubstep` (the 140 Croydon halftime original vs the brostep side, 138–142). Parser routing with guards for the ambiguous electro family (bare "electro" → Detroit electro; "electro house" → dancefloor; "electro pop/swing/hip hop" keep their lanes) and deep-dubstep placed above the generic \bdeep\b/\bdubstep\b word matches. Three compromised lanes upgraded onto real grooves (trance/armin entry driving→trance, juan atkins + drexciya driving→electro, big-beat/fatboy entry broken→bigbeat) + 14 researched artists (Prydz, deadmau5, Sasha & Digweed; Above & Beyond, van Dyk; Egyptian Lover; Dillon Francis, Major Lazer; Alok, Imanbek, Meduza; Skream, Benga, Digital Mystikz).
- **Gqom + dembow 2.0 (researched specifics)** — two grooves from web research: `house.gqom` (the Durban mutation with NO four-on-the-floor — broken syncopated kick answered by pounding log toms over distorted sub, sparse eerie chants, 115–128; DJ Lag / Distruction Boyz) and `house.dembowdom` (dembow dominicano — the raw Santo Domingo lane where every 16th is filled so it feels twice as fast as the tempo, 100–112; El Alfa / Rochy RD). Neoperreo (Tomasa del Real coined the term; Ms Nina) rides the classic `house.dembow` chop as the DIY deconstructed alias. Parser: "gqom" → house + own style; "dembow dominicano / dominican dembow" → dembowdom above the generic dembow style; "neoperreo / neo perreo" → dembow. The existing footwork/juke lane (RP Boo / DJ Rashad / Teklife, 155–165) pinned untouched.
- **Ghettotech + alté (researched specifics)** — `house.ghettotech` (Detroit's torqued-up Miami bass — banging 808 kick with syncopated booty-bounce pickups off the 0/8 grid, hard claps, 130–140, slower than the juke/footwork floor; DJ Godfather / DJ Assault / DJ Funk) and the alté lane (the Lagos alternative — R&B/soul-tinged afrobeats riding the afropop pocket, 90–108; Odunsi, Lady Donli, Santi). Parser: "ghettotech / ghetto tech" → own style; "afro tech" → the organic groove (placed above the generic \bafro\b match); "alte" → afropop with a genre entry (the word "alternative" can never hijack it). Gap fill: the g-house style phrase never existed — bare "g-house" used to fall into a random house pocket despite `house.ghouse` shipping.
- **Amapiano + organic house (researched specifics)** — two grooves: `house.amapiano` (the log drum genre — quiet four-floor kick never above 0.8 velocity, busy semiquaver shaker, syncopated rounded log-drum bass answering on the toms, 110–116) and `house.organic` (the Anjunadeep / Keinemusik wave — soft four-floor under conga-rim chatter, shaker and tom accents, 118–124). Parser: "amapiano / private school piano" → house + own style; "organic house / afro organic" → house + the existing organic style (bare "organic" stays unmapped so "organic ambient" still reaches ambient). Lanes upgraded: the amapiano roster entry (amapiano/rema/tyla) afro→amapiano 110–116 and Keinemusik (&ME/Rampa) afro→organic — Black Coffee deliberately stays on the classic afro-house groove. +5 artists (MDU aka Mas — "king of the log drum", MFR Souls, Daliwonga; Adam Port, HUGEL).
- **Rock lanes** — grunge, alternative rock, rapcore/nu metal and synth punk as house-family grooves with the ROCK-RADIO SONG FORM: `ROCK_FORM_STYLES` in the song builder gives these styles the verse / pre-chorus / chorus / bridge shape instead of the club intro/build/drop. Grooves: `house.grunge` (garage stomp — heavy 2/4 snare, driving 8ths, 95–125), `house.altrock` (tighter radio backbeat with syncopated kick, 85–125), `house.rapcore` (rap-syncopated kick under the rock backbeat with driving 16th hats, 90–120; RATM / Linkin Park) and `house.synthpunk` (punk tempo on machines — driving kick 8ths, tight and unswinging, 140–168; The Units). Every rock groove asserts the 2+4 backbeat in tests. Parser: "grunge", "alt rock / alternative rock", "rapcore / nu metal / rap metal", "synth punk" → house + own style (post-punk keeps its techno routing). +8 artists (Nirvana, Pearl Jam; Radiohead, Weezer; Rage Against the Machine, Linkin Park; The Units, The Faint). Reggaeton itself ships as `house.dembow` / `house.dembowdom`.
- **Metal / hardcore / punk / indie depth** — seven grooves joining the rock lanes under `ROCK_FORM_STYLES`: `house.metal` (the gallop — driving backbeat with kick pickups, 100–140), `house.thrash` (the driving 8th-kick beat, every eighth under the snare — asserted in tests, 140–180), `house.metalcore` (driving verses plus the HALF-TIME breakdown crush — same tempo, snare on beat 3, also asserted, 140–170), `house.doom` (the slow crawl — half-time, ≤2 kicks per bar, 50–80), `house.hardcorepunk` (the fast tight beat with a d-beat variation, 150–190), `house.poppunk` (the fast happy beat — kick 1 + and-of-2 push, 148–175) and `house.indie` (the garage-groove revival with offbeat open hats that make it dance, 100–130). Parser: "metalcore / thrash / doom (metal) / heavy metal / metal" (placed below the rapcore entry so "nu metal" keeps resolving to rapcore), "hardcore (punk)" — bare "hardcore" reads punk hardcore — "pop punk" and "indie / indie rock". +10 artists (Metallica, Slayer; Black Sabbath; Bring Me the Horizon; Black Flag, Minor Threat; Green Day, blink-182; The Strokes, Arctic Monkeys).
- **Melodic dialects (pilot)** — the lane identity finally reaches the melody: a per-style melodic layer (`MELODIC_BY_STYLE` in `melodic-data.ts`, keyed like grooves by `${genre}.${style}`, selected between the production profile and the genre fallback) with three pilot dialects — **amapiano** (the LOG DRUM as melodic content: syncopated short bass answering the kick, airy chord stabs, gentle piano lead), **dembow / dembow dominicano** (root-heavy chop bass answering the rim, bright stabs) and **metal / thrash / metalcore** (gallop bass, dark sustained power chords, dark minor runs). Without a style dialect the genre patterns stay byte-identical (golden baselines preserved); a dialect replaces the whole genre array and knows all three roles. Tests: dialect differentiation, aliases, genre fallback, determinism, key-safety. Wave 2 adds ghettotech (808 bounce stabs), baile (tamborzão punchy bass), footwork (jumpy off-grid polyrhythm + machine-repeated motif), jungle (THE chop bass — long deep sub under the fast break, patience asserted) and slap house (plucky 16th slaps with fifth pops).
- **Smart comping for arrangement take lanes (measured, evidence-first)** — `src/audio-engine/take-scoring.ts` + `src/commands/smart-comp.ts`, surfaced as **SUGGEST COMP** in the take-lane toolbar. Every source pass is MEASURED from its playable PCM on four axes with named thresholds: groove lock (onset spread around the take's own median, so the detector's shared pre-peak latency cancels and only per-take wander counts), pitch drift (autocorrelation f0 over 0.5 s voiced frames, gated on real energy so a periodic fan cannot score as "in tune"), noise floor (10th-percentile frame RMS) and clipping (hard penalty, the one thing a comp cannot repair). `planSmartComp` then gives each 1-bar window to the best take that actually COVERS it, merges adjacent same-winner bars (fewer seams = fewer audible crossfades), and reports uncovered bars instead of proposing silence; `applySmartComp` installs the whole plan as ONE undoable command over the already-verified `compAudioTakeRange`. SUGGEST COMP is a proposal: the card lists per-take evidence and the winning segments, CANCEL writes nothing, APPLY dispatches exactly one store command. Scope note: this plans the arrangement take lane from audio; `src/vocal/comping.ts` plans the vocal recording lane from already-measured `VocalProfile`s (phrase energy / SNR). The METRICS are deliberately separate (different inputs, questions and thresholds), but the PLANNING SPINE is shared through `src/shared/comp-core.ts` — best take per bar, merge same-winner spans, honest holes, earlier take wins ties — so the two lanes cannot drift on merge/hole semantics. `tests/comp-core.test.ts` (7 specs) pins that spine directly.
- **TSAR — hybrid sample+synthesis engine (23rd instrument, T0–T7)** — `docs/TSAR-ROADMAP.md` / ADR 0023. Per-sample AudioWorklet DSP core (`src/tsar/dsp/tsarProcessor.ts`, typed and vitest-testable): 16 voices (oldest-steal) with Source A + Source B (sample / wavetable / granular per slot), A↔B morph, sub + seeded noise, a per-source Chamberlin SVF (ported from the proven `svfilter-processor.js`, stability guard included), one ADSR per source, an 8-slot mod matrix (ENV/LFO/VEL/PRESS → A/B MORPH, A/B CUTOFF, MORPH, AMP, PAN) with per-voice decorrelated LFO phases, glide, velocity response, tanh drive, tone tilt, stereo width and a 1/√n polyphony divisor (16 voices at level 0.8 measured at 2.09 before). **Live↔offline parity is the architected core, not a fallback** (invariant #3): the runtime buffers every timed event and `AudioEngine.prepareOfflineRender()` seeds them through `processorOptions.events`, because Chromium does not pump worklet message queues during an `OfflineAudioContext` render — live (port) and offline (queue) share ONE `applyEvent` interpreter, pinned bit-identical by `tests/tsar/engine.test.ts`. The TSAR worklet is a LAZY instrument module (`INSTRUMENT_WORKLET_TYPES` / `instrumentWorkletTypesInDoc`, ~22 KB, own budget row in `check-bundle-size.mjs`) that a project without a TSAR track never fetches. A context without the module gets a real native subtractive fallback (audible=1 in the graph audit — 23/23), never a dead graph. **Sample Forge** (`src/tsar/forge.ts`, pure): YIN-style root detection (Goertzel-verified against the golden fixtures), one-shot/sustained classification (median-of-windows decay ratio, robust to AM), engine routing (sampler / wavetable / granular by stability), envelope + normalization, with honesty gates (unpitched → `rootMidi: null` + warning, silence → `kind: "empty"` which the command refuses). `forgeSampleCommand` applies a plan to slot A or B as ONE undo step. **Arpeggiator** (T6) runs per-sample in the DSP: UP/DOWN/UPDN/ORDER/seeded-RANDOM, rate/octaves/gate/swing, immediate first step on key press. **TsarPanel** (T4) is the dock editor (Alt+0): source/morph matrix/LFO/arp surfaces, Forge drop zone and the 48-patch factory browser — every control commits a command. Golden harness (T0) + DSP (17) + Forge (12+6) + presets (6) + arp (5) + panel (5) specs, all green; `docs/CURRENT-STATE.md` counts bumped (23 instruments, 610 presets).
- **Vocal comping + arrangement dialogue (vocal lane pilot)** — `src/vocal/comping.ts`: pure functions over measured `VocalProfile`s. `planVocalComp` assembles the best composite from ≥2 measured takes — per-bar scoring (phrase peak + bar energy + SNR bonus), consecutive same-winner bars merge into segments, gaps stay honest when nobody sings, per-take contribution and winner reported (`winner: number | null` — null when nobody sang, never a fabricated take 0). The planning spine is shared with the arrangement lane via `src/shared/comp-core.ts`. `applyVocalCompCommand` (`src/vocal/comp-apply.ts`) names comp tracks by TAKE INDEX so the mixer tracks, the summary line and the `VocalCompStrip` legend all agree on which take is "A" even when a later take wins the most bars (a rank-ordered naming silently let the top contributor claim the "A" track — pinned by a regression test); BUILD COMP is disabled when no bar has a sung take, so the button cannot throw on click. `suggestArrangementFromVocal` turns the take into arrangement dialogue: climax past mid-point → shorten the intro (late-hook), early climax → minimal intro, longest phrase gap → natural break/drop pocket, loudest bar → impact moment. Deterministic, honest (unmeasured takes get nothing). Harmony generation deliberately deferred until per-note pitch extraction exists.
- **UK funky / Baltimore / jungle (researched specifics)** — three grooves from web research: `house.ukfunky` (the London soca-bounce — broken kick punch + rim chatter + shaker, 125–130 swung; the "One Dance" sound), `jersey.baltimore` (the parent club sound — "Think"/"Sing Sing" break stomp with hard 2/4 snare and chopped-vocal blips, 125–135, slower than jersey club's 134–142) and `dnb.jungle` (the 1994 chopped-breaks original — ghost-snare chops + reggae sub + ragga pressure, 155–170, distinct from dnb.roller and dnb.amen). Parser phrases ("uk funky" carries its own style above \bfunky\b; bare "jungle"/"ragga" → jungle above the generic jungle→dnb style entry; "baltimore/bmore club" → jersey) + both existing Baltimore lanes (K-Swift/Scottie B and Rod Lee/KW Griff) upgraded from jersey.club/bounce onto the stomp groove + 4 artists (Roska, Lil Silva; Remarc, DJ Hype — Congo Natty/Shy FX stay on amen).
- **Legends + southern specialties + now wave** — 90s NY (2pac/biggie/wu-tang/jay-z/mobb deep), Dirty South founders (outkast/ugk/scarface/t.i./jeezy/gucci/mannie→bounce), 2000s mainstream (eminem/50/wayne/ross/dmx/busta/missy-timbaland), Bay/LA g-era (too $hort/quik/kurupt/yg-mustard/nipsey/blueface), three 6 + griselda on existing grooves; 5 new grooves (trap.bounce/miamibass/snap/countrytune, house.afroswing) with parser routing + artist lanes; female rap (nicki/cardi/latto/glorilla/sexyy/doechii/simz), latin trap (bunny/myke/duki/pnl), chicago/detroit/LA now, soundcloud era, death grips→dnb.amen; signature sounds (plugg bell, eski lead, syrup FX chain, electro snare, triggerman perc set); artist-name masking in text-parser (a name never doubles as a descriptor unless the name IS the descriptor, e.g. neurofunk).
- **DnB wave** — 29 artist presets across the whole genre tree (liquid: netsky / hybrid minds / high contrast / ltj bukem / dj marky; jump-up: turno / kanine / upgrade / a.m.c / serum; dancefloor: andy c / dimension / culture shock / metrik / grafix; neuro: noisia / black sun empire / phace / misanthrop / ed rush & optical / dom & roland; rollers: break / skeptical / alix perez / dilinja; jungle/ragga: congo natty / shy fx / general levy; two-step: roni size) with researched 160–178 BPM pockets; sub-genre parser sweep (jump up / drumfunk / techstep / darkstep / ragga jungle / halftime / minimal dnb / deep drum and bass — the last fixed to stop falling into house via the bare "deep" stem); context-gated `two step dnb` style so plain "two step" stays UK garage; guarded aliases (`serum` / `break` / `upgrade` only qualified — never hijack synth or arrangement talk); semantic corpus + description vocabulary (12 EN/SK runtime entries, 8 style synonym sets, dnb tempo words). Every style resolves to a real `dnb.*` groove id (`twostep` / `liquid` / `jumpup` / `roller` / `amen` / `dancefloor` / `neuro`).
- **Drum & Bass starter template** (kind #13 in `TemplateId`) — 174 BPM two-step roller with a Reese sub line and open-hat answers, registered in `TEMPLATES`, plus landing-page prompt routing (`liquid dnb 174` / `jump up jungle` forge from the dnb template).
- **DnB mix default** — `GENRE_TONE_DEFAULT.dnb = "dark"` (+2 dB master tilt) with mood/explicit-tone overrides still winning (liquid chill stays warm).
- **Techno depth wave** — the genre's biggest roster hole (24 presets vs trap's 140) closed with the floors that were missing entirely: **Detroit** (jeff mills / derrick may / juan atkins / kevin saunderson / carl craig / robert hood / octave one / terrence dixon + the soul axis omar s / moodymann / theo parrish), **dub techno** (basic channel / maurizio / rhythm & sound / deepchord / deadbeat / monolake / yagya — the `techno.dub` groove had zero artist consumers), **acid** (dj pierre / phuture / hardfloor / emmanuel top / luke vibert / tin man / 999999999 / nico moreno — the `techno.acid` groove had zero artist consumers), **micro-house** (villalobos / sonja moonear / perlon), **electro** (drexciya / dopplereffekt / dj stingray / helena hauff / aux 88 / client_03) and the **90s→now hard lineage** (dave clarke / ben sims / oscar mulero / dvs1 / dax j / len faki / speedy j / paula temple / shlomo). Guarded aliases keep generic words safe (`reese` = the DnB technique, `plug` = the English verb, `big beat` = promo text — all never match as artists). Parser gains `detroit techno` / `detroit electro` / `hardgroove` so they stop falling into the hip-hop "detroit rap" entry.
- **Experimental + score wave** — roadmap wave 12 edges (brockhampton / flying lotus / dälek / cLOUDDEAD / abstract hip-hop), neoclassical + modern-score ambient depth (einaudi / max richter / vangelis / steve roach / sakamoto / biosphere), big-beat + 90s-rave lineage (fatboy slim / chemical brothers / prodigy / orbital / underworld / leftfield) and the 2-step UKG originators (mj cole / artful dodger / zed bias / wookie).
- **House depth wave** — the genre's **founding history** was missing entirely. Added: **Chicago** (frankie knuckles / larry heard / mr fingers / marshall jefferson / ron hardy / steve hurley / farley jackmaster funk / chip e / adonis / ten city / jesse saunders), **Detroit house** (blake baxter / eddie fowlkes / kelli hand / terrence parker / dream 2 science), the **NJ/NY garage axis** (kerri chandler / masters at work / little louie vega / kenny dope / todd terry / tony humphries / basement boys / jovonn / dj spen / larry levan / paradise garage / david morales / danny tenaglia / francois k / joe claussell), **French filter house** (cassius / stardust / alan braxe / modjo / bob sinclar / martin solveig / breakbot + the darker gesaffelstein lane), the **modern deep/melodic school** (harrison bdp / fouk / braxton / jody wisternoff / anja schneider / maya jane coles / marsh), **amapiano wave 2** (musa keys / young stunna / de mthuda / sir trill), the classic **vocal/soul axis** (robert owens / adeva / barbara tucker / atjazz / osunlade / quintus) and the **disco/boogie wellspring** (giorgio moroder / nile rodgers / chic / sister sledge / arthur russell). Parser gains `chicago house` / `garage house` / `soulful house` / `filter house` / `ny house` (bare "chicago" deliberately stays with Chicago rap/drill). Guarded aliases: `justice` / `marsh` / `chic`-like generic words only match qualified forms.
- **Ambient / score depth wave** — the roster's thinnest genre (37) closed with the layers that were missing entirely: **4th world + minimalist avant-garde** (harold budd / robert fripp / larry fast / terry riley / la monte young / pauline oliveros), **drone / dark ambient / isolationist** (lustmord / sunn o))) / kevin drumsm / deathprod / svarte greiner / kammarheit / raison d'être / throbbing gristle / nurse with wound; `coil` qualified only), **new age + kankyō ongaku** (laraaji / constance demby / george winston / hiroshi yoshimura / midori takada / satoshi ashikawa), **minimalists** (philip glass / steve reich / michael nyman), the **score tradition** (morricone / badalamenti / john williams / howard shore / alexandre desplat + modern thomas newman / trent reznor / yann tiersen / jozef van wissem), **post-classical** (lubomyr melnyk / peter broderick / goldmund / dustin o'halloran), **electroacoustic + ambient revival** (caterina barbieri / sarah davachi / kali malone / kaitlyn aurelia smith / julianna barwick / ana roxanne / claire rousay), **experimental club** (moor mother / lotic / holly herndon — `arca` keeps its hyperpop entry), **glitch school** (ryoji ikeda / florian hecker / ben frost) and **breakcore / post-rock** (squarepusher / venetian snares / amon tobin / explosions in the sky / mogwai / sigur ros / godspeed).
- **UKG depth wave** — the genre had 14 entries and none of its history. Added the **1998–2001 originators** (so solid crew / oxide neutrino / groove chronicles / dem 2 / tuff jam / grant nelson), **speed garage** (todd edwards / armand van helden / 187 lockdown / double 99 / ripperman / baffled republic / tina moore), **bassline / Niche north** (ts7 / booda / paleface / witney / trc), the **dark 2-step corridor** (horsepower productions / benny ill / kode9 / loefah — El-B's lineage into proto-dubstep), **UK funky** (crazy cousins / appleblim; the house.ukfunky lane keeps roska / lil silva), **afroswing** (kojo funds / yungen), the **speed-garage revival** (main phase / badger / hamdi) and the **indie-adjacent crossover** (mura masa / joy anonymous / bakongo / north base). All styles resolve to the three first-class grooves `ukg.ukg` / `ukg.bassline` / `ukg.deep`.
- **User-request wave** — the last named asks: **LATIN MAFIA** (house/pop, 95–115) and **pluko** (trap/bouncy chill future-bass, 130–150); `overmono` / `duskus` / `Flume` / `PinkPantheress` were already covered. Five **new grooves** with parser routes and description/semantic vocabulary: `house.pianohouse` (122–130, bright piano-led floor), `house.midtempo` (90–110, half-time bass-music pocket), `house.breakbeat` (125–140, big-beat breaks), `ambient.sadchill` (70–90, swung emo lo-fi) and `ambient.dirtyambient` (60–90, corroded tape-degraded floor). All nine end-to-end cases resolve to real groove ids (`pianohouse` / `midtempo` / `breakbeat` / `sadchill` / `dirtyambient` verified through `resolveGrooveForGeneration`).

### Arrangement

- **MIDI clip multi-object note editing** — Ctrl/Cmd-click and Shift-range select arrangement MIDI clips, then open a context-menu editor to transpose, move, scale duration/velocity, edit note expression and slide flags, or clear note locks across the selected objects. One undoable command updates all selected source patterns. Selected clips sharing a source stay linked; when an unselected clip or scene also uses that source, the selected set is isolated through one cloned pattern. This is a batch transform panel, not a combined multi-source Piano Roll. Covered by `tests/midi-arrangement-clips.test.ts` and `tests/ui/midi-clip-lanes.test.tsx`.
- **Arrangement automation links (schema v30; ADR 0045)** — one automation clip can drive its primary target and linked targets. The curve remains stored in primary-target units and maps through normalized 0..1 space into each linked parameter's legal range; enum/toggle targets snap to a legal step. Arrangement lanes preview and edit the mapped curve. In Song mode, clips suppress pattern MOD automation for every linked target, and overlap precedence resolves independently per target (latest start wins, then lexical clip id); the playhead marks the winner and dims shadowed clips. Yjs round-trip, target normalization, command undo/redo, and per-target event resolution are covered in `tests/collab-automation.test.ts`; live scheduler dispatch and offline export mapping are covered in `tests/scheduler.test.ts` and `tests/renderer.test.ts`. Track/return/effect deletion removes invalid links inside the undoable command.
- **ArrangementPanel dekompozícia, 3/4 (2026-10-12)** — `src/ui/ArrangementPanel.tsx` **6 616 → 6 383 riadkov** (−233) bez zmeny správania. Tri collaboratori v `src/ui/arrangement/`: `ScenesPanel` (SCENES strip — create / dup pattern / variation / select / rename / delete / reorder / scene role / FILL-DROP-BREAK), `ArrangementViewControls` (ruler unit, intensity lane, zoom −/FIT/+), `ChordProgressionPanel` (GEN / ROOT / MODE / BARS / AT / PROG + ⚡ GENERATE). Fasáda `export function ArrangementPanel()` je nezmenená. Všetky tri sú čisté renderery — 0 hookov, 0 audio uzlov, 0 mutácií; stav a príkazy ostali v shelli. Dve cesty prešli do shella namiesto workaroundu: zoom clamping + FIT meranie (držia `MIN_ZOOM`/`MAX_ZOOM`/`scrollRef`/`totalBars`) a celý chord-generate vrátane `services.store.doc` + `execute()` + error cesty; `setChordGen` funkcionálne update-y nahradil `onChange(patch)` (vrátane `index: 0` resetu pri zmene žánru). `ViewControls` a chord generator vracajú **Fragment** (siblingi v jednom action row), `ScenesPanel` si necháva `arr-scenes` wrapper div. Brány: **studio boot path 2 116 KB → 2 116 KB** (nulová zmena; tri moduly rollup zlní do tej istej chunky), DAW JS 5 162 KB nezmenené, `tsc` bez chyby v `src/`, `drift:check` ✓ no drift. Testy: 18 súborov / 140 testov zelené po každom commite. Zostávajúce bloky a prečo potrebujú iný prístup viď nižšie.
- **Dlhé MIDI noty a 808 sustain (schema v28; ADR 0047)** — MIDI noty ostávajú riedke eventy a môžu presiahnuť 8-taktovú step mriežku až po bezpečnostný limit arrangementu. Piano Roll má horizontálne posúvanie a voľbu dĺžky novej noty 1/16–64 taktov; rozšírená fráza sa opakuje až na ďalšom takte po jej konci a MIDI klipy rovnakého zdroja sa rozšíria v tej istej undo operácii. Bubonové step polia ostávajú limitované na 128 krokov. Chromium overil 93-taktový/180-sekundový projekt cez IndexedDB save/reload a skutočný stereo WAV export; 12-taktový sustain má merateľnú energiu v neskorom takte. Plný 181-sekundový live soak s mute/move/resize/trim počas prehrávania prešiel. Dvojklik otvorí Piano Roll na zdrojovej fáze klipu; UI test potvrdzuje, že Duplicate je jedna undo akcia. Move/resize/trim/dvojklik E2E prešli v Chromium aj Firefoxe. Benchmark 300 klipov: 129 viditeľných pri otvorení, 105 na konci; mount 1 452 ms, end-scroll 244 ms. Windows WebKit sa preskočí, pretože jeho embedded engine nemá Web Audio.
- **Piano Roll Event Editor (schema v35; ADR 0049)** — integrated `EVENT` selection edits velocity and fine pitch for all internal instruments; a shared capability map populates `InstrumentDefinition.noteExpressionTargets` and drives the other event lanes. The Chromium and Firefox OfflineAudioContext sweeps pass 85 top-level checks over the declared runtime capabilities. The registry-derived live/offline parity matrix isolates each target/instrument pair and passed 157/157 in Chromium, Firefox and Edge in the fresh 2026-10-19 `npm run test:browser:note-expression:all -- --parity-only` run: velocity/fine pitch for 25 kinds, pan for 22, release velocity for 19, cutoff/resonance/Mod X/Mod Y for 16 filter runtimes, and held slides for 808/Acid. Pluck's recursive delay feedback now uses the sample-clocked `karplus-strong-processor` worklet, and the browser harness disposes each temporary engine after its capture. MIDI boundaries are explicit: KYX has no persisted FL-style note-color groups; external live pressure/CC74 is partial, keyed by pitch or latest note per channel and routed to the first instrument; live pitch bend is track-wide. MIDI-file import maps CC7/10/11 to arrangement automation and bakes CC64 sustain into note lengths; explicit release velocity survives pattern/song MIDI export, but automation controllers do not yet export back as CC events. Pitch bend, other CC, program change, aftertouch, SysEx and time-varying per-note expression remain unsupported. These are guarded by `tests/midi/midi-expression-boundary.test.ts` and `tests/parity-obligation.test.ts`. Windows Playwright WebKit has no Web Audio API, so Safari remains unverified. The 808 and Acid 303 support held-voice slide without a new attack; legacy `slide` remains portamento. **Open:** Safari/WebKit audio parity, note-color groups and full MPE/MIDI-file expression interchange.
- Arrangement-as-a-tool — variant swap, ripple edit, paint wiring. _(feat `2a7aa23`)_
- Finish the tool wave — drop-swap, multi-ripple, ghost preview. _(feat `49f678c`)_
- Determinism: velocityFx seedable, determinism verdicts recorded. _(feat `98e2e3b`)_
- **Independent AudioClip pitch (schema v37)** — audio clips store an undoable ±24 semitone offset independent of the clip's duration and tempo-fit rate. The Arrangement clip's Time & pitch dialog exposes the control; rendered pitch variants are cached and used by the shared live/offline trigger path. Duplicate, split, add and clipboard paste keep the setting. Covered by `tests/audio-clip-pitch.test.tsx`, `tests/clip-clipboard.test.ts` and `tests/offline-parity.test.ts`.
- **Live Pads / project-local Live Sets (schema v38; ADR 0055)** — each project can save up to 32 named 4×4 pad sets, duplicate and rename them, route each set to an audio-capable track, choose a 1/2/4/8/16-bar launch length, and drag browser samples onto pads. Outside arrangement capture, launches remain bar-quantized finite editable AudioClips. During capture, loop pads start and stop on bar boundaries; one-shot pads remain finite and may layer. FINISH or transport stop commits captured pad intervals with scene launches in one undoable command; CANCEL, pause, project replacement and audio-context loss silence or discard runtime voices. BPM-tagged pads use the existing pitch-preserving stretch path. Covered by `tests/live-pad-capture.test.ts`, `tests/live-sets.test.ts`, `tests/ui/LivePadsPanel.test.tsx`, `tests/arrangement-variations.test.ts` and `tests/audio-clip-pitch.test.tsx`. Capture is runtime-only until FINISH; the project schema does not change. KYX still has no bundled licensed loop or Live Set catalog.
- **Loop Browser tempo preview correction** — the existing user-loop browser filters by genre, mood, instrument, key and BPM range, and inserts a fitted, undoable AudioClip. Users can correct or supply loop BPM from 40–240 in the tag editor when detection is missing or wrong; that value drives the tempo filter, preview and fit. Preview playback uses target BPM ÷ source BPM; for tempo changes of at least 1%, preview and clip playback share the same pitch-preserving stretch renderer and cache, with source BPM ÷ target BPM as the duration factor. Below that threshold both paths use reciprocal resampling. A stretch-render failure also falls back to tempo-matched resampling, which changes pitch. The catalog is user-imported audio only; KYX has no bundled licensed music-loop pack yet. Covered by `tests/loop-tempo.test.ts`, `tests/ui/SampleBrowser.test.tsx`, `tests/ui/browser-strip.test.tsx`, `tests/preview-deck.test.ts` and `tests/persistence/UserSampleRepository.test.ts`.

### Export and interchange

- **Lossy share/web delivery — OGG (Opus) + M4A (AAC)** — the export menu is now three groups: _Master · lossless_ (WAV, FLAC), _Share / web · lossy_ (MP3 192/320, OGG, M4A) and _Social_ (VIDEO). Both new formats encode through the browser's WebCodecs `AudioEncoder` and are muxed into a real container by **Mediabunny** (`src/export/compressed-audio.ts`) — already a dependency for FLAC — rather than a hand-rolled OGG/MP4 writer. Encoding is **not real-time**, so a four-minute master takes seconds (unlike the MediaRecorder path behind VIDEO). The capability table (`src/export/compressed-audio-capabilities.ts`) checks `canEncodeAudio` at runtime through a dedicated lazy, named Mediabunny probe using the selected sample rate, channel count and export quality; the menu shows unavailable entries disabled with the reason instead of offering a format that always fails: **AAC encoding does not exist in Firefox on any platform, nor in any browser on desktop Linux**, and `AudioEncoder` only reached Safari in 26. Both formats are gated to 44.1/48 kHz — the same ceiling as MP3 — deliberately, while the FLAC encoder's wider table (to 192 kHz) stays unreachable from the UI because the renderer's DSP stack has never been tuned or tested above 48 kHz. Share formats **deliberately bypass the delivery-profile report**: `MasterFileFormat` and the Apple/streaming/vinyl verdicts describe what a _master_ must look like, and a web preview must never appear to satisfy that checklist. Mediabunny is imported dynamically so it stays out of the eager DAW bundle (same discipline as `flac-loader.ts`). Vitest covers the guards only — jsdom has no WebCodecs — so the real round-trip (encode → browser decodes the container back → RMS matches the encoded tone) is a browser check in `src/browser-checks.ts`; that check recorded `skipped: browser cannot encode codec` rather than passing on browsers that cannot produce the format.

- **One audio-import policy, enforced on every path (`src/audio-import-capabilities.ts`)** — the acceptance rules for importing audio previously lived in **four files with three different lists** and one constant that described itself as the single source while importing it **nowhere**; no test pinned any of them. KYX could export a container its own library then refused to re-open. Three findings, all now closed: (1) the documented 25 MB ceiling was enforced on **1 of 3** paths into the shared decode — `importAudioFile` read the bytes and called `decodeAudioData` with no bound, so the SEND-TO-KYX intake tray (base64 straight off the collab server, materialised ~3× in the main thread before any check) and the TSAR forge picker were both unbounded; the ceiling now lives inside `importAudioFile`, which every caller reaches, and `base64ToAudioBytes` refuses an oversized network body from its **encoded** length before `atob`. (2) The dead constant is gone and the list is now read from one module by DropZone, the intake tray, TSAR and the Reference Map panel — including the file-picker `accept` attribute and the visible hint text, which had been out of step with what was actually accepted. (3) `.m4a` / `.aac` / `.webm` / `.oga` / `.wave` / `.aif` are accepted, closing the round-trip against the OGG/M4A export above. **The import gate is decode-driven and is deliberately WIDER than the export menu**: browsers decode AAC/M4A nearly everywhere (Firefox included, via platform codecs) even where they cannot _encode_ it — importing must never be narrowed to match what KYX can write. Locked by `tests/audio-import-capabilities.test.ts` (17), including a tripwire that replaces `arrayBuffer()` with a failing sentinel to prove the ceiling fires **before** the read rather than after it, an export→import round-trip assertion that would have caught the `.m4a` asymmetry, and single-source guards. The external-mastering surfaces (`MasteringFileSessionPanel`, `MasteringReferenceCompare`) are deliberately **exempt**: they take only WAV/WAVE/MP3/FLAC and keep their own 128 MiB / 320 MiB ceilings because they master full-length mixdowns, not one-shot samples — a test says so explicitly so nobody folds them into the 25 MB sample policy.

- **BWF Broadcast Wave metadata** — deliverable WAV exports (master, grouped stems, per-track stems, scorepack master/stems, ZYVO transfer) carry an EBU Tech 3285 `bext` chunk: originator, timestamp, sample-accurate time reference and — wherever a render summary exists — the measured loudness in the spec's v2 fixed-point fields (LUFS-I, LRA, true peak, momentary/short-term), which Pro Tools / Nuendo / film workflows read on import. Opt-in at the encoder level (`createBextMetadata` in `src/rendering/wav.ts`), so internal round-trip WAVs (freeze, bounce persistence, consolidation, Qvester handoff) stay byte-compatible. Spec-pinned by `tests/wav-bwf.test.ts`.

- **Text-to-mix, closed loop — `kyx_mix_idea`, the 34th tool (2026-10-04)** — one natural-language mix request runs the whole loop the existing pieces only implied: `kyx_mix_idea {idea: "warmer and glue the drums"}` returns a PLAN of concrete device steps with the WHY attributed (targets + parsed goals + per-step device/params, nothing applied — planning folds commands over local docs only), then `apply: true` lands the whole idea as ONE undo step, and the report points at the verify loop (`kyx_render_summary` for measured WHY, `kyx_blind_ab` plan/record/verdict for the human ear). Built on the two existing interpreters in FxIntentBar precedence: the PRODUCTION concept planner (warmer/punchier/brighter/glue/air/notch… — SK and EN — with sentence-level targets incl. pad families and per-lane "mix", `applyProductionIntentCommand` = one snapshot) and the ASSISTANT's curated goal language scoped to a track (`applyEffectIntentOnTrack` find-or-add + plan, folded across tracks into one snapshot). Explicit `target` (family word or track id) overrides the sentence; planner refusals ("no matching track") surface as honest plan failures, never mutations. `preview: true` closes the listening half: `src/mcp/mix-preview.ts` renders the BEFORE/AFTER masters (plan folded over a local doc — still nothing applied), measures both with the mix-doctor BS.1770 loudness, computes a ±12 dB-capped level-match playback gain and arms a session lane the studio card picks up (`MixPreviewChip`, lazy — ▶ Before / ▶ After for the human, APPLY executes the stored command as one undo, DISMISS throws it away); desktop-stdio arms the lane where the UI cannot bind it and the tool says so instead of pretending. Module `src/mcp/mix-idea.ts` (lazy, mirrors mix-diagnosis); mirrors regenerated; locked by `tests/mcp-mix-idea.test.ts` (11: no-mutation, one-command apply, target override/scoping, mix→all-leaf resolution, assistant guidance refusals, report framing) + 2 dispatch flows in `tests/mcp-tools.test.ts` (34-tool surface).
- **Gallery battles — the blind A/B taste tournament (2026-10-04)** — published beats face off in random pairs, visitors vote blind (A / B / tie / both-weak), and every vote accumulates as real human preference data: the taste-data flywheel, collection phase. Server: `BattleStore` in `server/collab-server.mjs` (`gallery-battles.json` beside the gallery store) — an append-only vote log (cap 50k) + Elo ratings (K=32, base 1000; both-weak logged but rating-neutral), same-genre pair pools (≥4 beats) when available, and one vote per unordered pair per anonymous session (409; the dedupe index rebuilds from the log, so it survives restarts). Blindness is server-side by contract: `GET /api/gallery/battles/pair` carries ONLY ids + codes; titles/authors/provenance exist first in the vote response's reveal. Routes: pair / vote / leaderboard (top 10 by battle-earned Elo) / export (pairs + outcomes WITHOUT voter sessions — the ranker-training log; fine-tuning the candidate ranker on it is the documented next wave). Client: `src/gallery/BattlePanel.tsx` renders the blind arena (two embed players sharing an `exclusiveGroup` — new EmbedApp prop, one beat plays at a time), reveal + Elo movement after the vote, TOP BY BATTLES list; `galleryApi` gained pair/vote/leaderboard + a stable `pf-battle-session` voter id. The flywheel surfaces in the product itself: every feed item carries its battle-earned Elo and `GET /api/gallery?sort=battles` orders the feed by it (never-battled beats follow; default stays newest-first) with a `⚔ TOP BY BATTLES` chip and `⚔ elo` card badges in the gallery. Locked by `tests/gallery-battles.test.ts` (9: blind shape, Elo movement, tie/both-weak math, dedupe + restart persistence, leaderboard order, session-free export, Elo feed ordering) and two battle flows in `tests/ui/GalleryPage.test.tsx`.
- **Interactive beats — the embed ENERGY slider (2026-10-04)** — `/embed` share pages render a dark and a bright ENERGY variant of the project in the background (offline passes over a transformed clone: velocity scaling toward ghost notes + per-family track gains — drums/bass keep the skeleton, pads/leads fade, one-shot FX die first; documents with an authored scene-intensity rig ride their own rig instead). All three buffers play in sample-locked sync through per-variant gains and the slider equal-power crossfades between adjacent anchors, so the listener rides the beat from skeleton to drop with zero re-renders and byte-parity at the default position (0.5 = authored mix). The same surface is exposed over a `kyx:*` postMessage API (play/pause/toggle/seek/energy/get-state + throttled `kyx:state` + `kyx:energy-ready`) for games and OBS overlays, plus streamer hotkeys (↑/↓ ±10 %, 1/2/3 anchor snaps) on the standalone share page. Inline embeds (landing hero, gallery cards) stay single-render with no slider; beats longer than 7 min or <2 GB devices report the control as unavailable instead of stalling. The share hash accepts a deep-linked level (`#p=<code>&e=73` boots the slider at 73%; junk degrades to the authored 0.5 — `initialEnergyFromHash`). Core math in `src/embed/energy.ts`, locked by `tests/embed-energy.test.ts` (14).

### Windows audio I/O

- **Coordinated browser multitrack capture (2026-10-19)** — `PcmMicRecorderGroup` coordinates up to eight distinct browser input-device IDs onto unique arrangement tracks. Every recorder prepares before the shared AudioWorklet start frame is released; startup failure cancels the group, runtime device failure stops its peers, and committed audio remains recoverable. Each track can use dry software monitoring, and completed clips share one undoable placement. Engine and UI setup/failure paths are software-tested. The native WASAPI session still exposes one capture endpoint at a time; independent device-clock drift correction, physical connector mapping and Volt 276 qualification remain open.

- **Independent-input software regression recheck (2026-10-20)** — Eight focused `PcmMicRecorder`/group cases passed: ungrouped capture exclusivity, same-frame start, distinct devices and takes, isolated per-track monitoring, batched durable chunks, Stop during preparation, partial-preparation cleanup with a late permission result, and peer recovery after device failure. The partial-prepare test confirms that an already prepared member does not start, its empty recovery session is removed, and the pending member's late stream is closed. A focused `ArrangementPanel` test also passes the unique track/device controls and verifies that the secondary track's monitor toggle stays independent of the armed input monitor. These synthetic checks do not measure physical clock drift or Volt routing.

- **Native Windows shared-WASAPI path (2026-10-19, ADR 0050)** — KYX Studio can select exact input/output endpoint IDs and channel maps, connect the isolated utility host to the live AudioEngine master with bounded PCM packets, and send native capture into arrangement recording. Packets cross Electron as copied `ArrayBuffer`s with sequence acknowledgements and fixed-pool recycling; this is bounded but not zero-copy. The shared engine keeps offline rendering on its regular offline graph. Software checks cover packet handling, loss cleanup, exact endpoint requests, channel mapping, shared periods and bounded SPSC rings. A fresh Chromium OfflineAudioContext smoke verifies six of six non-zero AudioWorklet render packets and six capture packets through the public worklet, with zero pool drops and successful recycling; a recorder test injects native `device-lost` during a take and confirms committed PCM is preserved as recoverable. These checks do not exercise the live AudioEngine master graph, Electron utility, physical unplug or WASAPI. Current limits: shared float32 at the AudioContext sample rate, mono/stereo capture, stereo output, no independent-device drift correction. Two 30.2 s direct native-session soaks on active Creative Live! Cam Sync capture + MSI MP273Q render endpoints (48 kHz / 480-frame period, silent output) started in 234/285 ms and each accepted 3003/3003 writes. They recorded 0/1 render underruns, 0 capture overruns, 1 capture discontinuity per run, 0 timestamp errors and 0 non-finite samples. Maximum capture/render queues were 480/1920 frames; each 1056-frame endpoint buffer equals 22 ms at 48 kHz. The adapter surfaced no positive `GetStreamLatency` values. The first integrated utility/worklet smoke did not qualify: one render underrun, one capture discontinuity and only two capture packets in 30 seconds; a later startup-handshake run stalled beyond 20 seconds. Neither run used AudioEngine master audio, and captured samples were not saved. The Volt 276 was absent during the endpoint check; integrated-path stability, physical channel identity, analog round-trip, disconnect/reconnect and 30-minute hardware soak remain open. See [ADR 0050](./adr/0050-windows-audio-io-direction.md) and the [support matrix](./WEB-STUDIO-SUPPORT-MATRIX.md).

- **WASAPI startup recovery (2026-10-19)** — Native snapshots expose the active startup phase and endpoint. A 7-second utility watchdog now sends that phase to the renderer, then exits only the isolated audio utility if WASAPI startup is still blocked. On a post-recovery integrated attempt with the active MSI output and Creative Live! Cam Sync input, the watchdog reported `capture/starting-audio-client` after 7.17 s; the utility exited with code 1, AudioSrv remained running, and active endpoints enumerated again. This bounds recovery but does not make capture startup pass. A later smoke found its selected capture endpoint inactive before stream initialization. By 11:26, Windows logs showed four AudioSrv AppHang/unexpected-termination events at 10:14, 10:58, 11:11 and 11:26; one cross-process report references `audiodg.exe`. At 11:32:39, after the automatic service restart, metadata-only enumeration found AudioSrv running and active Creative/Realtek capture plus MSI/Realtek render endpoints; shared periods were 480 frames except Realtek render at 512. A fresh metadata-only enumeration at 12:36 +02:00 again found AudioSrv and AudioEndpointBuilder running, those four active endpoints at 48 kHz float32, and every Volt endpoint `not-present`. Both reads opened no stream. This confirms enumeration recovery only: no stream was opened and the root cause remains unknown. The later 20:31 AudioSrv AppHang is recorded below.

- **Volt 276 reappeared; shared-duplex Start hangs reproduced (2026-10-19)** — A later read-only PnP and WASAPI metadata check found active Volt capture/render endpoints at 48 kHz stereo float32, channel mask `0x3`, with a 480-frame shared period. Two bounded 10-second simultaneous shared-mode probes exceeded the 25-second timeout; the instrumented repeat showed capture blocked in `IAudioClient3::Start()` after initialization, MMCSS and event-handle setup, while render blocked in `IAudioClient3::Start()` after initialization and silent-buffer priming. Both owned child processes were terminated and confirmed gone. Capture PCM was discarded and render sent silence. AudioSrv and AudioEndpointBuilder remained running, `audiodg` reported responding, endpoints stayed active and no new related Application/System warnings or errors appeared. No further stream was attempted; physical jack mapping, product-path stability, round-trip latency and unplug/reconnect remain unverified. The timeout wrapper now captures child logs, and the probe flushes per-call phase markers.

- **AudioSrv AppHang after the Volt Start hang (2026-10-19 20:31 +02:00)** — Application Hang 1002 and WER `AppHangB1` identify `svchost.exe_Audiosrv`; Service Control Manager 7031 says Windows Audio had terminated five times and scheduled a restart. The service was running again under a new PID. WASAPI metadata marked all Volt endpoints `not-present`, while PnP still listed the input endpoint as `OK` and did not list a present render endpoint. A bounded 3-second capture-only probe failed at active-endpoint enumeration before `IAudioClient3` activation, so no stream opened. This followed the two `IAudioClient3::Start()` hangs but does not prove they caused the service crash. Root cause and recovery remain unverified; no more physical streams were attempted.

- **Volt endpoint enumeration recovered after AudioSrv restart (2026-10-19 20:36 +02:00)** — A later metadata-only WASAPI query again found active `INPUT 1/2` capture and `MONITOR L/R` render at stereo 48 kHz float32 / 480-frame shared period. A full PnP read marked the Volt device and both endpoints `OK` with no problem code. No service was manually restarted and no stream was opened. This confirms enumeration recovery only; `IAudioClient3::Start()` still hangs in the preceding probe, and physical mapping/latency/dropout recovery remain unverified.

- **Standalone Volt capture `Start()` remains blocked** — After that endpoint recovery, a bounded 3-second capture-only probe reached `capture-start` but did not return within 10 seconds; its owned process was terminated. It did not reach the capture event loop or read PCM, and did not open render. AudioSrv remained running, no new related events appeared, and follow-up PnP/WASAPI metadata still showed both endpoints active. This reproduces the capture `Start()` hang without a simultaneous render start; cause and physical mapping/latency/dropout remain unknown. No further stream was attempted.

- **Current Windows audio state (2026-10-20 05:43 +02:00)** — A read-only Application log query still finds the 2026-10-19 20:31 `AppHangB1` for `svchost.exe_Audiosrv` as the latest AudioSrv hang; no later Application Hang 1002 or WER `AppHangB1` was present through this check. `Audiosrv` and `AudioEndpointBuilder` are running. PnP lists `Universal Audio Thunderbolt WDM` as `OK`, but the metadata-only WASAPI endpoint list marks every Volt capture/render endpoint `not-present`, with no active mix format or shared-period data. The matching WER `ReportArchive` directory denies reads, so its report payload could not be inspected. No stream was opened; this is not evidence that Volt streaming recovered.

- **Windows audio endpoint inventory (2026-10-20 06:16 +02:00)** — A fresh metadata-only WASAPI `--list-json` query again marked every Volt capture and render endpoint `not-present`. Creative Live! Cam Sync and Realtek Array capture endpoints, MSI render and Realtek render endpoints remained active at 48 kHz float32; shared periods were 480 frames except Realtek render at 512. No audio stream was opened. The previous AudioSrv log check remains the latest service-state evidence; this inventory alone does not show Start or duplex recovery.

- **Native duplex startup cancellation (2026-10-20)** — If Stop arrives while capture `IAudioClient3::Start()` is delayed and that call later succeeds, startup now stops capture before it can start render. Stop during render startup stops both clients, and the startup result cannot commit `running` when Stop won the completion lock. The C++ regression checks cover those timings, cleanup after either endpoint's `Start()` failure, and preserving a failed exact-endpoint state plus its original error after Stop, all without opening an endpoint; all four CTest targets pass, with nine duplex lifecycle scenarios. The Electron manager now waits for the utility's `exit` event after forced termination before starting a replacement; if termination is not confirmed, it keeps the old session recorded and rejects the replacement. Window-close and app-quit paths log unconfirmed shutdown instead of leaving a rejected promise unhandled. Nine manager tests pass. A `Start()` call that never returns still depends on the isolated utility watchdog; these changes do not diagnose or fix the AudioSrv hang.

### Reference Map (audio analysis)

- **Mix Doctor (mix QA)** — `src/analysis/mixDoctor.ts`: pure, deterministic mix-health analyzer that runs on every MASTER export and surfaces a one-line verdict in the export panel (`MIX CHECK PASS — low 62% · crest 12.4 dB · peak −1.8 dBFS` or the flag list). Measures the exact dimensions the 2026-09 sound-library audit used to diagnose broken generated beats: band energy shares (RBJ biquad cascade on the mono downmix), sub+low dominance, crest collapse, clipping/over-full-scale, DC, stereo phase, BS.1770 loudness. Red flags = the measured pre-fix disaster zone (clip / low-end > 82 % / crest < 6 dB / DC ≥ 1 mV / L-R phase inversion); yellow = advisory (legitimately low-heavy trap/drill sits ~74–78 %). Diagnoses only — auto-correction via gain staging is a deliberate follow-up, keeping the determinism invariant (a pure function of the rendered buffer, never a time-varying pass).

- **Reference Map panel (F4)** — `src/ui/ReferenceMapPanel.tsx`, reachable from the topbar as **REF MAP** and from the bottom dock. Drop a finished track (WAV / MP3 / FLAC / OGG / M4A / OPUS, 25 MB ceiling enforced _before_ decode) and read back tempo, key, Camelot code and a High/Moderate/Low confidence word, with the half/double reading a producer actually argues about, per-file BPM + key/mode correction, and JSON export. Tabs: MAPA (onset envelope, beat markers, F2 section bands, energy strip) / RYTMUS / HARMONIA (12-bin chroma + top-5 key candidates) / DIAGNOSTIKA / MATCH.
- **MATCH tab — "ako ďaleko som od referencie" (reference-matching wave)** — `src/reference/match.ts` (pure, deterministic, byte-identical on identical input): renders the current pattern **pre-master** (the match measures the MIX, not the master's reaction — the match-eq precedent), measures both sides through the **same** analyzers (mix-doctor band shares, BS.1770 loudness, mid/side stereo width), and reports the deltas in the mix-doctor's 7-band vocabulary as dB-of-own-total shares — a loudness-invariant domain, so a −20 dB reference compares honestly against a loud mix. The table names WHERE the difference lives (e.g. "your air sits 8.4 dB above the reference"), the loudness half reads in LU, and the stereo half is qualitative (a width number is perceptual, not a figure to chase). **Per-track attribution** renders each content track (≤14) as its own pre-master stem and ranks band ownership energy-weighted (loudness × band share — the same math the agent's mix diagnosis uses), so a gap names its owner ("Kick owns 58% of Sub · the mix is 3.1 dB thin there"); a muted owner gets an unmute reason instead of a dead fader move, and a band with no ≥40% owner gets no strip action (an ownership guess would be an invented target). **APPLY MATCH** folds the master match-EQ curve + loudness trim + every unmuted per-track fader move into **one undo step** — the master half rides the existing 4-band master EQ stage (`applyMasterMatchEqCommand`), the strip half rides `setTrackParams`, so no new DSP or mutation surface. Four honesty rules, each earned by a real number: the reference's own descriptors are **never** a target (only the measured _difference_ is a suggestion, the F2 §2.2 rule); nothing auto-applies (the button is the only path to a command); a sub-1.5 dB gap reads "—" not "0.0 dB"; and a **participation floor** (a band is empty only when quiet on BOTH sides, judged against each side's own loudest band) stops a dead band from winning on filter leakage while still catching a band one side genuinely lacks. The **GROW owner strip** (wave 2): when the Low-mid (120–350 Hz) gap is a harmonics job — the mix carries the sub body while Low-mid is thin — and the band owner is an unmuted instrument strip, the fader move is superseded by a device proposal; APPLY MATCH then inserts (or re-tunes) GROW on that strip with measured starting values (presence scaled from the gap, tilt from the sub-vs-Low-mid shape, 0.05-grid rounded) inside the SAME one-undo snapshot. Locked by `tests/reference/match.test.ts` (20: contract, level-free shares, ±6 clamp, empty-band floor, share semantics, ownership ranking, muted-owner/no-owner guards, one-snapshot apply, determinism, hostile input, the GROW owner strip: trigger shape + starting values + re-tune-never-duplicate) and `tests/ui/reference-map-panel.test.tsx` (7: tab surface, measurement, attribution, resonance detection, APPLY disabled without a measurement, one command per apply, the GROW owner strip rendering + APPLY arming). _(feat `cc72f28a`, `02d196a1`)_
- **Problem frequencies — per-track resonance detection (resonance wave)** — `src/reference/resonance.ts` (pure, deterministic): the per-track companion to the match. For each content stem it runs an averaged periodogram (the same FFT recipe the intent layer's `findResonances` ships) and finds narrow peaks that beat a **rolling-median local floor** (so a legitimately bass-heavy track does not flag every low band), adding the two numbers an EQ cut actually needs: **Q** from each peak's −3 dB bandwidth (clamped to the native EQ free band's 0.1–24) and a **bounded cut** (0.7× the prominence, clamped −12 dB). A flat spectrum yields **zero hits** — no invented problems. The MATCH tab's "Problem frequencies" section lists the offenders per strip ("120.0 Hz · 11.3 dB over the local floor · Q 3.8 → cut 7.9 dB") with a per-track **CUT** button that notches them on the native EQ's two free surgical bands (`free{1,2}Type=BELL` + freq + gain + Q), reusing an existing `eq` instance or adding one, all in **one undo step** (`applyResonanceCutsCommand` → `addEffectWithLandingCommand` / `setEffectMacroParams`). This is the deterministic "zníž rezonanciu 120 Hz" the production intent layer already half-shipped (`src/intent/production.ts` free-band notch), now measurement-driven per track. Locked by `tests/reference/resonance.test.ts` (11: right frequency, prominence order, flat-noise zero-hits, bounded cut, existing-eq reuse, two-band cap, hostile input, determinism) and the panel suite (1: detection + CUT one-undo). _(feat `103a3416`, `2b163c73`)_
- **Acts on the project (F4-full)** — `src/reference/apply.ts` turns a ReferenceMap into commands through the existing `setBpm` / `setProjectKey` / `setGroove` paths, so nothing invents a second source of truth. The panel can set project BPM, set the project key hint, and import markers as a **single undo step**. The marker import uses the F2 **section map** when one was found — a `drop` section becomes a `drop` marker, not a generic cue — and falls back to equal-length phrases for a signal with no detectable structure. Groove feel is a deliberate **user** value: F1 does not measure swing, so the panel says so rather than showing a fabricated number.
- **Structure + descriptors (F2)** — `src/reference/structure.ts` and `src/reference/descriptors.ts`, ported from the `beat_modifier` analysis pipeline. Structure: a 16–64 point RMS energy curve plus a section map (intro / drop / breakdown / outro / development) with edges **snapped to the F1 beat grid**, so an imported marker lands on a downbeat. Descriptors: spectral centroid / rolloff / flatness / band balance / brightness, LUFS-ish integrated level with peak, crest and dynamic range, mid/side stereo width measured from the **original channels** (a mono downmix has no side component and honestly reports 0), transient density and syncopation with a groove family, and a plain-language summary that is the F5 Inspiration builder's input. These are **descriptors, not mastering targets** — clipping is reported as a warning and never corrected. Three deliberate departures from the Python source are documented in the modules: beat-snapped sections, short-section merging, and a 60 dB dynamic-range ceiling (the raw `p95/p10` formula measured 223 dB on a click track, where the 10th percentile is digital silence rather than a musical level).
- **Deterministic engine (F1)** — `src/reference/`, browser-native, no server and no model. Spectral-flux onset envelopes → comb-ACF tempo candidates → beat grid → Krumhansl–Schmuckler rotated-Pearson key scoring, all in a Web Worker with a transparent main-thread fallback. Same file → same BPM/key/Camelot every time; silence and unusable input report honest `null` + a warning rather than an invented number. The export separates `detected` from `confirmed` so a user correction never overwrites what the engine actually found.
- **Not yet shipped:** F3 groove/swing extraction (the analyzer does not measure feel yet) and F5 inspiration generation.

### Architecture

- **Plugin delay compensation waits out the graph's latency — including on a DRY track (2026-10-22)** — the compensation delay sat AFTER each chain, but a chain with NO effects connected straight through and owned no delay node at all, so the compensation `syncPdc()` computed for it had nowhere to go. A dry track therefore landed **2.5 ms (the gate's look-ahead) EARLIER** than the gated one — the browser verifier measured it exactly: `alignedDelta = 120 samples (a=574.0, b=694.0)`, byte-identical on a clean baseline checkout, so it was a long-standing misalignment, not a regression. The 2026-10-17 optimization (ADR 0048 — "100 delays for 100 effect-free tracks") made the node lazy, and the dry chain is EXACTLY the chain that waits out someone else's latency, so it was the one that got nothing. The node is now allocated **on demand**: a chain that needs a non-zero compensation gets one (`ensurePdcDelay`), and a project whose graph carries no latency anywhere still pays for no delay nodes at all. Locked by `tests/pdc-offline.test.ts` (11 specs — three of them new: the dry chain owns a delay once it has to wait one out, the delay sits between the chain's tail and its sink, and a graph with no latency allocates none).

- **External file mastering (2026-10-07, bounded session; ADR 0022)** — `MasteringFileSessionPanel` imports WAV/MP3/FLAC mixdowns into an independent local IndexedDB session, stores an immutable Blob plus SHA-256 and bounded 50-step config undo/redo, then runs the shared `renderProject()`/`AudioEngine` master chain through an isolated scratch document and SampleBank copy. Its full master insert rack edits only the session config. Named A/B snapshots store complete settings; both versions render against the same source and audition can attenuation-match the louder version without altering either render. A session-local, separately stored read-only WAV/MP3/FLAC reference is hashed, measured and auditioned against the rendered master with preview-only loudness matching; it is excluded from render/export. Delivery creates 16/24-bit PCM or 32-bit float WAV with BWF loudness metadata, checked 16/24-bit FLAC, or MP3; FLAC uses a bounded seekable chunk target with a 96 MiB file cap, and its additional encoded-file/Blob memory estimate must fit the 512 MiB working-set guard. The encoded result is inspected before download, with new-session re-import supported. The session report shows LRA, loudness timeline, stereo metrics, crest and Mix Doctor findings from decoded WAV/FLAC where available; otherwise it marks the source-PCM view and preserves the post-encode `not measured` reason. After inspection, the user can download a JSON sidecar tied to the source SHA-256, config revision, profile, source-PCM analysis and encoded-file inspection; it labels source-only verification when post-decode audio was not measured and includes no audio. Up to six 2 MiB JSON sidecars now persist locally across workspace reloads; deleting a session removes its reports. Limits: 96 MiB source, 0.8 s–12 min, mono/stereo, decoded source ≤128 MiB, render working-set estimate ≤512 MiB. Disk streaming and cross-browser duration/memory soak remain open. Chromium E2E covers UI cancellation during source/A-B render, WAV encoding and final inspection; it also covers source/reference Blob reads, hashing and decoding, reference worker analysis, cooperative source PCM audit and session restore/retry, plus source/reference quota errors, database permission failure and malformed WAV-header rejection. A further Chromium E2E verifies two-track album batch delivery at 44.1 kHz as 24-bit WAV, 24-bit FLAC and 320 kbps MP3; six decoded files have JSON reports, gain-only matching targets −22 LUFS-I under a −1.5 dBTP guard, saved mastering configs remain unchanged, and current album metrics update. Separate Chromium gates confirm cancel and stale-order changes during first render stop at 0/2 deliveries without downloads or reports. Five focused Firefox E2E scenarios pass: the complete external WAV delivery round-trip, source/reference quota errors, storage permission failure and source/A-B render cancellation. Windows Playwright WebKit has no `AudioContext` and is verified only against the graceful Web Audio fallback; Safari macOS remains an owner gate.
- **FLAC source streaming acceptance (2026-10-10)** — Chromium imports a four-second stereo FLAC larger than 512 KiB through bounded 256 KiB Blob reads, verifies exact frame count and PCM agreement with KYX's whole-file FLAC decoder (maximum sample difference below `1e-7`), reloads the saved source from the local session, and rejects a damaged audio frame without saving a partial session. The targeted E2E passed **1/1**. Safari/macOS and additional browser/OS checks remain open.
- **MP3 source streaming acceptance (2026-10-10)** — Chromium imports a four-second 320 kbps stereo source through the two-pass WebCodecs decoder, matches the declared frame count, and reaches >`0.95` correlation against native browser decode after alignment. It rejects a damaged interior frame, restores the saved session and confirms cancellation terminates the worker during either pass (**2/2** focused E2E scenarios). Safari/macOS and additional browser/OS profiles remain release gates.
- **Master limiter transient/recovery linking (2026-10-10)** — project and external-file sessions expose separate advanced 0–100% controls for transient gain reduction and release recovery. Both default to 100%; schema v20 maps the v19 combined link to both fields. The final true-peak safety correction remains linked, and the UI warns that lower values can shift the stereo image. Native fallback does not support these controls and reports Degraded.
- **Master limiter character voicings (2026-10-10)** — schema v21 adds Transparent (the existing default response), Punch and Smooth to project and external-master controls. Character changes the knee and gain envelope only; all modes retain the same final linked true-peak correction and ceiling. Synthetic transient tests confirm distinct envelopes and finite output under the ceiling; controlled level-matched listening remains open, so these are choices rather than a quality ranking.
- **Studio HQ master limiter oversampling (2026-10-10)** — Studio HQ render-only mode runs the enabled master limiter at 2× through a 129-tap half-band FIR up/downsampler; LIVE remains on the existing 1× path and limiter bypass does not add oversampling. The FIR cascade adds 64 base-rate samples, reported by the limiter runtime and included in PDC. Tests confirm impulse latency, DC preservation, lower folded products for a nonlinear test signal, and decoded true peak under the ceiling for hostile vectors at 44.1/48/96 kHz. This is an engineering check, not a listening verdict; musical level-matched listening and broader CPU/render-time checks remain open.
- **Mastering report history recovery (2026-10-09)** — users can clear persisted external-delivery JSON sidecars without deleting the owning mastering sessions, source audio or references. This also provides a recovery path when a saved sidecar is damaged or uses an unsupported schema. Each history row shows its save time and originating session revision for provenance.
- **Master monitor-tone tail isolation (2026-10-09)** — live post-limiter master recording and A/B/reference/assistant audition are blocked while the 440 Hz signal is active and for the estimated decay window of active master inserts. The tone is refused while transport plays and stops if playback starts during the signal. The estimate uses the offline renderer's tail policy (2 s fallback, 12 s cap); a visible countdown follows the live audio clock and a polite announcement marks completion, while second-by-second visual updates stay hidden from screen readers. Reopening MASTER during the window announces the restored wait once. The audio engine stops existing preview audio before scheduling the test tone after its de-click fade. A new test tone is also refused during the window. This is a time estimate, not an acoustic silence detector, so the manual checklist still requires confirming the audible tail has decayed. Track/return tails are excluded because the tone enters the global master chain directly. Manual listening on a controlled output remains open.
- **Master insert latency visibility (2026-10-09)** — MASTER Signal path shows per-insert runtime latency and distinguishes reported values, pending worklet reports, runtimes that do not report latency, and effects without a ready runtime; a pending zero is never shown as zero latency. Composite ZENIT runtimes aggregate sub-stage readiness and latency-change subscriptions so the offline-render barrier and PDC receive reports from nested worklets.
- **Workspace grid regression fixed (2026-10-11, editor audit D14)** — `src/styles/24-layout-tokens.css` re-declared `.workspace` with only TWO grid tracks. Because that file is imported LAST in `src/styles/index.css`, the later same-specificity rule silently replaced the three-column template from `20-control-kit.css:472`, so grid auto-placement dropped `.workspace-main` (TrackTabs + PatternBar + **Sequencer**) into the 280 px inspector column. Measured in Chromium against the production build at 1280×720 / 1366×768 / 1440×900 / 1920×1080: `.workspace-main` 280 px wide and `.sequencer` **256×20** with 618 px of content scrolling inside a 20 px window at EVERY viewport; at 1280×720 the pattern bar overflowed into the dock band so `elementFromPoint` on the GEN button returned `BUTTON.dock-resize-handle` and the flagship GENERATE action was unclickable (specs 03/04/05 failed on that click). Fixed by scoping the rule to `@media (min-width: 1101px)` — a media query adds no specificity, so an unscoped rule in the last-imported file beats every earlier breakpoint, but a `min-width` range that never overlaps theirs cannot. One owner per range, no duplicated ladder rung, and `tests/layout-responsive.test.ts` (which forbids 24-layout-tokens.css from restating the ladder) stays 15/15. Same treatment for the `.user-label-width` rule (`@media (min-width: 761px)`), which had been killing the 760 px `--steps-label` phone breakpoint for users with a custom label width. Core suite 01–08 against the production build went **8 passed / 5 failed → 15 passed / 1 failed**, the one red being `07-group-routing`, which imports `/src/*.ts` and can only run against the dev server. Full detail, measurements and falsification runs in `EDITOR_QUALITY_AUDIT_REPORT.md` (wave 10 / D14).
- **Playback verified in a real browser (§17, 2026-10-11)** — `tests/e2e/20-playback-master-bus.spec.ts`, Chromium, production build: plays the House template and reads the master strip's own peak readout (`.master-channel-peak-readout`, fed by `MasterStereoMeters` straight from the engine's master stereo snapshot). Four independent runs: transport `1.1.2/1.1.3 → 8.2.3 / 9.2.2 / 9.2.3 / 9.4.4`, master peak **−1.3 dBFS** every time with samples moving between ≈ −9.5 and −1.3 dBFS, track meters peaking at 100, and **−∞ on the master after STOP** — no stuck voice, no ghost audio. The spec asserts the bus is already silent BEFORE the transport press, so the measured signal is provably caused by playback rather than ambient meter state, and it scopes its console-error filter by origin: refused `http://127.0.0.1:1234/api/intake` (collab server, not running in this environment, different origin) is tolerated, a refused **same-origin** asset stays red.
- **Heap stability measured across lifecycle cycles (§35, 2026-10-11)** — `tests/e2e/21-heap-stability.spec.ts`, Chromium, production build: ten identical panel open/close cycles with `HeapProfiler.collectGarbage` before every reading and a warm-up phase first, so the delta is live retention and not uncollected garbage. Two runs: baseline 15.29 MB → growth **−0.22 MB**, baseline 16.40 MB → growth **−0.24 MB** — flat, no per-cycle retention in the panel lifecycle. The 4 MB bound is derived from the measured ±0.6 MB noise band (≈7× headroom, catches ~0.4 MB per cycle), not chosen in advance.
- **Short viewports give the editor the space (2026-10-12, editor audit D15)** — `src/styles/25-short-viewport.css`. Measured at 1280×720 before this: the workspace column was 349px and spent 151px on the pad rack and 149px on the pattern toolbar, leaving the **sequencer 20px** — the editor the app exists for (at 1366×768 it was ~68px). The studio read as "pad grid, four rows of buttons, and a mixer". Owner's call: **the editor has priority**. `.workspace-main > .sequencer { min-height: 150px }` (outside any media query — the priority is the rule at every height), `.workspace-main > .pattern-bar { min-height: 72px; overflow-y: auto }` so the toolbar yields instead of vanishing, and below 820px tall the pad rack drops to one row while the dock stops at 34vh. Below 860px wide the editor floor relaxes to 90px because the inspector becomes a ROW and shares the budget. After: sequencer **150px at 1280×720 / 1366×768 / 1280×800**, nothing clipped (`.app` overflow delta 0), and 1440×900 / 1920×1080 unchanged (218 / 467px, two rack rows, toolbar not scrolling). The `.rack` rule lives in `24-layout-tokens.css` beside the `.rack` rule it belongs to, and `25` is imported BEFORE `24`: `tests/layout-responsive.test.ts` pins both "24 must not define its own 860px block" and "24 must stay the last stylesheet", and two earlier attempts to place these rules elsewhere turned that guard red. Costs, stated plainly: below ~820px tall the dock cannot be dragged past 34vh, and a one-row rack truncates pad names ("Kick Deep" → "Ki…"). Locked by `tests/e2e/19-workspace-layout.spec.ts` (9 specs; falsified against the pre-change CSS, which measures 20px at 720 and 57px at 768).
