- **A crash card no longer outlives the panel that caused it (2026-10-12, editor audit D16)** — dock slots render their occupant by POSITION, not by remount: `App.tsx` renders `renderDockSlot(dock.slotA)` inside `.dock-slot` with no `key`, so React reuses the same `ErrorBoundary` instance and only updates its props. `hasError` therefore survived a panel switch, and a mixer crash re-presented as **"devices crashed" carrying the mixer's error text** — the new panel blamed for an error it never threw. `ErrorBoundary` now forgets a previous crash when the guarded subtree's identity changes (`componentDidUpdate`; `resetKey` defaults to `panel`, so all 21 existing call sites are fixed without edits, and `resetKey` covers the same label over different content such as one rack following another track). The four `TopBar` popovers are safe already — they sit inside `{open && …}` and unmount wholesale; the dock slot was the only swap-without-remount site. Measured and now pinned: **Retry is a clean remount, not a resume** (`count 2` → crash → Retry → `count 0`), and route mode offers reload only, never Retry. One measurement invalidated one of my own tests: React 18 in DEV **replays a failed render** (a child that always throws is invoked 5× per `render()`; a throw-on-first child is invoked 4× and **never crashes at all**), so any attempt-counter inside a throwing child is a worthless probe — all flaky children in `tests/fault-containment.test.tsx` are driven by an external flag instead, and one test pins the trap so it is not walked into again. That replay also means `componentDidCatch` fires 4× for one logical crash, so the "card is kept" test measures child invocations rather than log counts. The chunk-load classifier now has all four patterns plus four near-misses (a miss tells users to Retry a cached rejected promise; a false positive sends them to a full reload and loses unsaved work). `attachProcessorErrorGuard` idempotence is pinned, since it only holds while the handler is a property assignment. `tests/fault-containment.test.tsx` went 7 → **22 tests**; every guard was falsified against a deliberately broken build (reset removed → 2 red; `handleRetry` no-op → 3 red; classifier matching everything → 7 red). `npx tsc --noEmit` is back to **0 errors** — the red on `tests/e2e/14-recording-soak.spec.ts` recorded in wave 14 was fixed by the recording session, with no edit from me.
- **The boot path can no longer go silent (2026-10-12, editor audit D17 + D18)** — two defects in the gap between "the user decided something" and "the user sees a result". **D17 (P1):** `Boot` in `src/main.tsx` awaited `import("./services")` with no timeout, and `fetch()` has no timeout of its own, so a stalled or service-worker-starved chunk left the user on _"KYX — forging audio engine"_ with no error and nothing to click. Measured in Chromium against the production build: healthy boot reaches the project browser in **1596 ms**; with the `services` chunk never fulfilled the loader appeared at **968 ms** and the Reload button **never appeared**. The IndexedDB-blocked variant of this bug is NOT present and was not reported as one — `persistence/db.ts:41-103` already caps a blocked open at 5 s and rejects with _"Database is locked by another KYX tab"_. The fix is a deliberate **warning, not an abort** (`BOOT_WARN_MS = 15_000`, ~9× the measured healthy boot): it renders only while `screen.kind === "booting"`, reuses the existing `.crash-screen` classes (no new CSS), and names the three real causes. Measured after: stalled chunk fires at **16.1 s** with an actionable screen, healthy boot **1976 ms** untouched, and a chunk delayed 18 s **still lands** — that third case is the falsification of the design, since a warning surface is only safe if boot may finish afterwards. **D18 (P2):** `openDoc` was fire-and-forget while `ProjectBrowser` released its `busy` guard as soon as the click was dispatched, so the entire studio build ran behind a fully interactive project browser — zero feedback for the click, and a window in which a second click started a second `openProject` whose loser was never `closeProject()`-ed (no final save, `AudioContext` left open). `openProject` only awaits lazily under `?collab=` (the ~300 KB yjs runtime is deliberately out of the boot path), which is the honest way to measure a large window: with the collab chunk delayed 6 s the browser left the screen in **194 ms** after the one-line `setScreen({ kind: "opening" })`, versus still present after 2 s without it. Locked by `tests/e2e/22-boot-watchdog.spec.ts` (3 scenarios) and `tests/e2e/23-project-open-feedback.spec.ts` (2), both falsified against reverted builds. Honest caveat on D18: the double-open is a **latent** race — React's synchronous flush on a discrete click usually unmounts the browser before a second click lands, so the _measured_ defect is the missing feedback and closing the race is defence, not a fix for an observed loss.
- **The crash banner no longer calls every ordinary exit a crash (2026-10-12, editor audit D19)** — the Project Browser banner fired whenever the journal did not end on `session-close`, and `session-close` is written in exactly one place: `closeProject` (`src/services.ts`), which only runs when the user clicks **back-to-browser inside the app**. Closing the tab, following a link or refreshing never writes it, so the overwhelmingly common case of a deliberate exit was reported as "Previous session may have ended unexpectedly (crash or force-quit)." Measured in Chromium against the production build: open a template, navigate away, reopen → last journal event `session-open`, banner visible, crash wording. The app asserted a cause it cannot know, and a user who learns the banner cries wolf stops reading the one warning that would have mattered. **An unload-time journal marker was tried and removed**: instrumenting both handlers with a synchronous `localStorage` write alongside the IndexedDB one showed the hook fires but the IDB write dies with the document, so `session-exit` was dead code. Since the app cannot know why a tab went away, the copy now claims only what is known — "The last session did not end through KYX's normal shutdown — you may have closed the tab or followed a link, or the app may have been interrupted." The banner still appears (hiding it would silence genuine iOS terminations, which fire the same `pagehide`, and the module's contract is "may over-report, must never under-report"); it just stops naming a cause. `CrashJournalReport` carries that warning on `lastSessionClean` itself so the next reader cannot repeat the mistake. `tests/e2e/24-crash-journal-false-alarm.spec.ts` (2) + `tests/crash-journal.test.ts` (18).
- **The unload save flush never saved anything — now fixed by a synchronous recovery buffer (2026-10-12, editor audit D20, P1)** — `installSaveUnloadGuards` exists so an edit inside the 800 ms autosave debounce survives a tab close, and it answers **both** `visibilitychange`→hidden and `pagehide` with `flushSave()`. Measured in Chromium against the production build, reading the persisted document back out of IndexedDB (the journal's own `save-ok` marker is _also_ an IDB write, so it cannot distinguish "save lost" from "marker lost"): BASELINE template untouched = **0.95**, CONTROL click + idle 3 s = **0** (saved), UNLOAD click + close tab = **0.95** (**the edit is gone**). Both unload events provably fire (synchronous markers `{"visibilityHidden":…662,"pagehide":…660}`), so the guards are not being skipped — **neither IndexedDB transaction commits before teardown**. The user closed a tab and their last edit vanished silently, and the safety net gave a false sense of protection; real exposure was bounded by the autosave window (`debounceMs: 800`, `maxDeferMs: 5000`) at roughly **5 s of work**. **Owner's decision: the recovery buffer, not a shorter autosave window** — so write frequency is unchanged and the ~4× IndexedDB amplification was not paid. `src/persistence/recoveryBuffer.ts` keeps the uncommitted document in `localStorage`, the one store whose writes provably survive unload, synchronously end to end. It is written from a new synchronous `onUnload` hook that runs _before_ `flushSave`; cleared the moment a save commits, and on recover/discard, so a stale buffer can never shadow newer work; offered by the Project Browser only while `isBufferWorthOffering` holds (buffer newer than the saved project's `updatedAt`), and recovering is a real `repo.save` plus opening the studio rather than a one-shot view, so a second crash keeps the work; and it degrades silently to today's behaviour when storage is unavailable, over quota, or the document is not JSON-serialisable. **Predicate bug caught by measurement, not by reading:** the first version gated the write on `saveStatus` being `dirty`/`error`, and instrumenting the handler showed the real status at pagehide is **`"saving"`** — the debounce had already started the write that dies with the document — so it wrote nothing in exactly the case that loses work. The rule is "protect unless nothing is uncommitted" (`saveStatus !== "saved"`). Verified after the fix: buffer present (9969 B) → stored step still 0.95 before recovery → `[Recover]` → **0**. `tests/recovery-buffer.test.ts` (10) + `tests/e2e/25-unload-save-flush.spec.ts` (3, incl. BASELINE/CONTROL fixture). **Also recorded:** the first D20 assertion was `expect(step).toBeGreaterThan(0)`, which **passed against the defect** (the click toggles the step off, so the untouched 0.95 satisfies `> 0`); it now compares against the measured baseline.
- **Mastering workspace interaction QA (2026-10-06)** — `tests/e2e/17-mastering-workspace.spec.ts`, Chromium **6/6 PASS**: injects a 1 kHz tone through wet and bypass master paths, keyboard-activates signal-flow navigation to TILT, checks visible MIX/DEV/EXP and Alt+1/Alt+9 shortcuts, verifies no 390×844 horizontal overflow, round-trips a reference Blob through browser IndexedDB and deletes it, confirms canceling a master render leaves no report/download, then runs full-song analysis → stale-on-INPUT-edit → re-analysis → downloaded 16/24-bit PCM and 32-bit float WAVs. Every export has measurable peak/RMS energy, BWF v2 read-back, decoded audio measurements, and a frame count matching the render report. The injected tone caught and fixed a missing connection between the two master TILT filters.
- **Master monitor-bypass scheduling (2026-10-06)** — `tests/master-chain.test.ts`, **8/8 PASS**: verifies 15 ms wet/dry ramps with automation hold, re-sync after a delayed latency value, the 0.999 s alignment cap/warning and exact delay writes for offline preparation. Browser audio/click-free acceptance is still open.
- **Master insert export tails (2026-10-06)** — `resolveRenderTailSeconds()` now includes active processors in the global master insert rack as well as track/return FX. `tests/render-quality.test.ts` covers VØID reverb decay, feedback delay, and bypassed inserts so the offline render does not truncate a master-bus effect tail.
- **Offline render allocation guard (2026-10-06)** — `renderProject()` checks the estimated stereo Float32 output against a 320 MiB ceiling before constructing `OfflineAudioContext`; unknown and over-budget estimates fail with an actionable error rather than attempting a browser-sized allocation.
- **Gate look-ahead/PDC correction (2026-10-06)** — the gate worklet now delays its stereo audio by exactly the fixed 2.5 ms it reports to PDC, with the same physical latency when the look-ahead switch is off. It previously delayed only the gain control, so the reported latency did not describe the audio path. `tests/effects-wave1-upgrade.test.ts` checks exact per-channel delay; the browser offline-export alignment probe now measures 0 samples of dry/gate skew.
- **Markers are navigation, not arrangement content** (fix, 2026-09-28) — `normalizeProject` used to clamp every marker to the project's current length on every command. On every shipped template that length is 4 bars, so cues placed at bars 8/16/24/32 were silently stacked on bar 4 with no error. The normalize pass now only sanitizes marker _shape_ and keeps ticks non-negative; the intentional shrink-clamp stays in `markerClampPatch` (`src/commands/docOps.ts`), where it lands inside the command delta so undo restores the original ticks. Pinned by `tests/marker-position-regression.test.ts`.

- **ADR 0012 — MRT2 generative tracks** records the Mac Apple-Silicon-only generative-tracks helper path; the helper builds in `native/mrt2-host/`, packaging lives in `electron-builder.yml` and `desktop:build:mac:mrt2`. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gates its downloadable QA artifact on the shared test suite and packaged-app smoke verification.
- **ADR 0013 — Windows generative companion tiers** records the separate Windows manager/transport, capture-first capability tiers, benchmark gate and fixed optional companion/model paths. The checked-in JAX capture host, opt-in WSL2/CUDA near-realtime launcher, SHA-256 package manifest verifier and guarded model-data uninstall script live under `companion/mrt2-windows/`, `desktop/` and `scripts/`; the macOS helper remains Apple Silicon-only. The 2026-09-25 RTX 3060 Laptop 600 s WSL2 stream failed promotion (35.98 ms p95, 9 overruns), so Windows live playback is still experimental and is not advertised as promoted realtime.
- **Pro-DAW standards waves 1–3** (ADR 0016–0018) — BWF `bext` metadata on deliverable WAVs (EBU Tech 3285 v2 loudness fields); CLAP plugin **scanning** out-of-process (`npm run build:clap-probe`, MIT 1.2.10 headers vendored with SHA-256 manifest, real-probe acceptance against compiled fixtures); MIDI Timecode in (`src/midi/smpte.ts`, correct 29.97 drop-frame math, statusbar readout, BWF timeReference bridge); ASIO driver **discovery** (`vendor:asio` + `build:asio-probe`, headers-only Steinberg fetch behind the license gate) and **streaming** (`asio-host` → ADR 0018 PCM frames, acceptance-tested against the SDK sample driver as a 64-bit fixture DLL — realtime-paced, seq-contiguous; real-hardware soak = owner gate); **renderer playback components** (SPSC SharedArrayBuffer ring `src/audio-engine/pcmRing.ts`, Node mirror and `pcm-playback` AudioWorklet pass same-process component tests). The desktop bridge is **not shipped**: Electron 44.4.3 rejects SAB transfer through both `ipcRenderer.invoke` and `webContents.postMessage`; see ADR 0018/0050 for the measured boundary and viable in-renderer native-addon spike. MTC **transport chase** is shipped (ADR 0017 wave 2: `MtcChaser` maps received timecodes to the timeline at the current BPM — full frames jump immediately, playing drift re-syncs throttled, armed from the MTC chip). CLAP audio hosting and resampling remain unshipped — see the ADR support matrices.
- **Robustness Wave 1 — crash journal** (`src/persistence/crashJournal.ts`) — session/save lifecycle events (session-open / save-ok / save-fail / session-close, capped 50) recorded in the meta store; a journal not ending on `session-close` fingerprints an unclean shutdown (crash / force-quit / tab termination) and the Project Browser shows a dismissible banner with the last completed save. Journal writers are best-effort and never fail the save/open/close they observe; the data layer stays the single-transaction `ProjectRepository.save` (project body + recent pointer commit together). Locked by `tests/crash-journal.test.ts` (14 specs).
- **Robustness Wave 2 — PDC export parity** — the engine's minimal PDC (`AudioEngine.syncPdc`: per-track/group/return compensation delays + per-send delays, live latency reports via `onLatencyChange`) gains an **offline export barrier**: `renderer.ts` calls `engine.prepareOfflineRender()` right before `startRendering()`. It settles the worklets' async latency reports (main-thread tasks the render does not wait for), sizes the graph, then writes the compensation delays with exact `setValueAtTime` (the live path keeps its click-free `setTargetAtTime` glide — on a fresh offline timeline that glide left the head of every export with a look-ahead chain progressively misaligned for ~100 ms) and locks the graph against mid-render `syncPdc` mutations. Latency-reporting effects: gate (2.5 ms), limiter (lookahead 1–20 ms plus 32-sample output true-peak correction, plus 64 samples in Studio HQ), tape (8 samples), fxeq/ultina/ozvena/morph (oversampling, reported by the DSP); the remaining effects are per-sample zero-latency by design. Locked by `tests/pdc-offline.test.ts` (4), `tests/renderer-pdc-settle.test.ts` (4, source-grep) and the browser-check E2E (gate track vs dry track land within 2 samples in a real offline export + reported-latency control).
- **Mixer signal-flow verification (2026-09-30)** — `docs/MIXER-VERIFICATION-2026-09-30.md` traces the real graph (track/group/return node chains, group re-parenting via `routeDestination`, the fixed master chain) and repairs **one confirmed defect**: `syncSends()` removed a send by disconnecting only the send's own output, never the upstream `modMacroPan → sendGain` edge (Web Audio `disconnect()` severs only outgoing edges), so every send off/on cycle left an orphaned `GainNode` + `DelayNode` pair live in the graph and stacked extra send contributions into the return bus — measured 3 → 8 channel-output edges over five cycles. Full-channel teardown was never affected (`disposeTrackNodes`/`disposeGroupNodes` disconnect `modMacroPan` wholesale), which is why deleting a track stayed clean and the toggle path did not. Locked by `tests/mixer-send-leak.test.ts` (5: single removal, five-cycle accumulation, group/bus parity, **group-delete re-parenting to the master with no orphaned route**, full-teardown guard). Gain/pan/send clamping verified consistent across command, schema and UI layers; channel metering reads the channel's own post-fader analyser; group mute/solo preserve member state; mixer state round-trips through persistence unchanged. **Multiple simultaneous solos** (two groups at once, a soloed child in each group, a group plus a loose child, releasing one of two, group mute under a concurrent solo) are now pinned directly in `tests/groupTracks.test.ts` — previously every case drove the audibility matrix with a single solo. Nested groups and pre-fader sends are **not** supported (documented, not defects). Three pre-existing failures in `tests/master-finish.test.ts` (2) and `tests/reliability-hardening.test.ts` (1) are **not** from this work — proven by stash-and-rerun baseline.
- **Intent engine end-to-end verification (2026-09-30)** — `docs/INTENT-VERIFICATION-2026-09-30.md` traces both AI systems (the 14-command recipe bridge in `src/ai/bridge/` and the generative pipeline in `src/intent/`) and repairs **two real defects plus the guard for their class**. **D-2**: the bridge's `COMPRESSOR_RANGES` mirror had not picked up the DE-ESS params (`scMode`, `scBandHz`) the compressor registry gained, so every bridge-written compressor carried two ids its own validation surface did not know. **D-3 (the serious one)**: the `msEq` effect was redesigned from a 3-band crossover into a 4-band M/S EQ (mid/side × low/high), but `applyMidSideEq` still wrote the **old** ids (`lowFreq`, `comp`, `solo*`, `mix`, …) — all ten were dead keys stripped by `normalizeEffects`, so a "scooped" M/S EQ request produced a correctly-typed, undoable, correctly-labelled effect with **every gain at the registry default**: a validated command that did nothing. Root cause in both cases is the same — the `*_RANGES` tables are hand-maintained mirrors of `EFFECT_DEFS`, and the specs asserted against the _mirror_ rather than the effect, so spec and bug agreed and the suite stayed green. Repairs: `MSEQ_RANGES`/`MidSideSpec`/`midSideSpec`/`applyMidSideEq` remapped onto the live 4-band surface (MID carries tone, SIDE carries the width intent), `ms-eq` validation now range-checks every band centre, and `COMPRESSOR_RANGES` + `CompressorParamId` gained the DE-ESS pair. **D-4** closes the class: `tests/ai/bridge/param-mirror-parity.test.ts` asserts coverage, no invented keys, exact min/max/default agreement and default-in-range for all seven range tables plus `EQ_BANDS`; `applyBridgeCommandForTest` was added beside the existing `__validateBridgeCommandForTest` so specs can assert the document a single command really produces. `tests/ai/bridge/` 95/95 PASS. Verified sound: the bridge mutates the **domain model** only (never UI state), validate+apply run in one pass over a working copy so a failed multi-step batch cannot leave a half-applied project, the whole batch is one undo entry, and failure is a typed code (`no-recipe-match` / `no-track-match` / `invalid-command` / `project-saturated` / `validation-failed`) — no silent guessing.

- **Preset loudness map: the VSCO hole closed (2026-10-04, same day as the seam)** — the loudness-measurement harness fetched every pack sample from `/samples/piano/` regardless of pack, so every VSCO id 404'd, its render measured silent fallback, and the preset silently dropped OUT of `preset-loudness.generated.ts` — 0 dB normalization gain plus a drift gate that skips ids the map does not know. The catalogue was never heard of by the mastering pass (the 2026-09 "everything at target" convergence covered core presets only). Fixed path per pack prefix and regenerated: +38 VSCO entries (gain range −14.5…+18, engine-path drift gate now active for all of them — 562/562 QA PASS), core entries byte-stable (495 ids, 0 changed — measurement parity), one new clamp-hit pinned (`factory.vsco.sviolinpizz`, solo pizzicato vs the pluck-family median). Follow-up root cause (same day): the earlier +38 left 29 VSCO presets unmeasured — NOT an engine divergence. The harness fetched ~2 400 sample WAVs with one naive `Promise.all`, the dev server dropped a random subset under the burst (whole instruments 0/44, timpani 26/28), and any dropped sample rendered its preset silent. The harness now loads packs through the SAME bounded ensure-queue the studio apply path uses (`ensurePianoPackLoaded`/`ensureVscoPackLoaded`) — **562/562 presets measured, zero unmeasured**, core entries byte-stable across re-runs, one more clamp-hit pinned (`factory.vsco.timpani`), drift gate active for the whole catalogue (562/562 QA PASS).
- **Preset-pack lazy seam (2026-10-04)** — the real-instrument preset DATA got the same contract its samples always had (optional, on demand): `presets/factory.ts` now carries the core curated bank only; the Salamander piano + VSCO2 orchestra presets (their generated velocity/keyzone tables, ~290 KB built) load through `presets/pack-presets.ts` inside the existing `warmFactoryPresets()` — same full bank for every loader consumer (preset intents, MCP list/load, browser QA), nothing packs-aware changed at the call sites. Effect: DAW JS chunk total 4025 → 3769 KB (the 4010 budget holds with 241 KB headroom; the bump ladder's debt repaid), tracked as a new `optional preset packs` budget bucket. PresetBrowser/browser-checks/loudness measurement read the bank through the loader; `tests/preset-factory-loader.test.ts` + the 10 preset suites pin the merged-bank contract. Deliberately NO manualChunks forcing — it hoisted a preload edge into the landing graph (+298 KB); the natural dynamic chunk stays truly lazy.
- **VSCO 2 CE real-sample packs (2026-10-03)** — Salamander piano (120 samples, 30 keys × 4 velocity) + VSCO2 orchestral subset (163 → 452 samples: violin/cello ensembles, flute, clarinet, french horn, harp, glockenspiel, marimba, **real percussion kit** GM-map 220 regions incl. 9-velocity snares + RR, **upright piano** 69 regions) — CC0/CC-BY 3.0, on-demand packs git-ignored (converters: `scripts/convert-salamander.mjs` / `convert-vsco2.mjs`; SFZ-region parser), `SampleLayer.root` multisample keyzones (engine), presets `Real Piano (Salamander)` + 8 VSCO presets via `velocityLayers` in InstrumentPreset; license registry `src/sample-library/licenses.ts` + credits gate — see docs/SAMPLE-SOURCES.md.
- **Pitch Correct — scale-snap vocal correction, the 48th core effect (2026-10-02)** — the last hole in the vocal chain (record → comp → tune → harmony → de-ess → mix) closed with a real-time AudioWorklet: a YIN-style CMND pitch detector (control-rate, ~21 ms updates, clarity-gated ≥ 0.85) drives the same COLA dual-voice granular engine as the pitch shifter at a smoothed correction ratio. Params: RETUNE (amount), SPEED (4 ms hard tune ↔ 150 ms natural glide — block-rate smoothing coefficient per checklist #4), KEY (C..B) + SCALE (chromatic / major / natural minor), MIX. Zero port messages (offline render identical to live), deterministic (no randomness). Locked by `tests/pitchcorrect-worklet.test.ts` (detection golden vectors incl. the octave-error case a pure 330 Hz tone used to trip + scale-snap math) and the host-level browser check (RETUNE min/max moves the measured Goertzel peak from Eb to E over a C-major target — the checklist #13 min/max contract). The 42 → 43 core / 47 → 48 registry counts are re-measured in this commit.
- **Clavinet was silent for its entire shipped life (fixed 2026-10-01)** — the `clav` factory built a complete voice chain (square + saw → panners → highpass → bandpass pickup → `amp`, plus a tine partial, growl sub and seeded tangent click) and the `clock.onended` cleanup even called `amp.disconnect()` — but **`amp.connect(output)` was missing**, so the chain dead-ended and every note rendered exact silence. Symptoms were all pre-existing and measured: `defaultAudible: false`, `defaultPeak: 0`, all 9 params "dead" in `docs/PLUGIN-AUDIT-2026-09-27.md` (row marked **FAIL**), and every `factory.clav.*` preset auditioning at `peak=0.0000 rms=0.0000`. The 2026-09-27 audit found it and never fixed it — its "Loads" column said PASS while the signal column said FAIL, and nothing closed the gap. The audit's own report JSON proves `clav` was the **only** instrument with `defaultPeak === 0` (`spectral` is the next lowest at 0.0003 ≈ −70 dB and is a separate, much weaker question). Fixed by the single missing line; locked by `tests/instrument-voice-reachability.test.ts` (4: registry-slice coverage, dead-amp detection, output feedback-loop, and the specific Clavinet pin). The pin is **verified falsifiable** — deleting `amp.connect(output)` again fails it with `clav lost amp.connect(output) — the Clavinet is silent again`. The audible half remains the generic render loop in `src/browser-checks.ts` (`<Name>: noteOn renders signal`, `peak > 0.01 && peak <= 4`) which runs for all 22 kinds — a regression there would have caught this, which is exactly why the FAIL row was never closed.
- **Host-level DE-ESS gate (2026-09-30)** — closes the one real gap the plugin matrix carried: `compressor.scMode` / `scBandHz` landed one day after that audit's render sweep, so its columns had no evidence for them. New real-browser check `compressor: DE-ESS mode ducks the sibilance band, not the low band (host)` in `src/browser-checks.ts` renders through `EFFECT_DEFS.compressor.factory` (not a direct `process()` call, so a wrapper that drops the params cannot hide) and asserts in-band discrimination plus that moving `scBandHz` off the probe **releases** it. **PASS** in Chromium at 44.1 kHz: duck 0.3112 in-band vs 0.2743 with the band moved (`released=0.0370`), sibilant/low ratio 0.120. Verified by execution, not inspection — the first two drafts were wrong and only real runs caught them (one reused a single `OfflineAudioContext` for three probes, which throws on the second `startRendering`; one asserted HPF mode spares the sibilance band, which is false — a 20 Hz high-pass passes 6.5 kHz in full). Full chromium gate: **285/294**; the 9 failures are all outside the audit's scope and all pre-existing (clavinet `noteOn` silence, preset loudness drift, RR variant derivation, template count 14 vs 12, perf budgets, artifact gate, app boot, plugin E2E timeout).
- **Instrument signal-graph verification (2026-10-03)** — `docs/INSTRUMENT-VERIFICATION-2026-10-03.md` replaces the regex source-pins with real graph reachability over all 22 `INSTRUMENT_DEFS`. `tests/helpers/graphAudioContext.ts` is a Web Audio mock that registers edges on both endpoints, makes `disconnect()` sever outgoing edges only, and separates audio edges from control edges (LFO → depthGain → `bandpass.frequency` is two hops). Every source is classified into six buckets — `audible` / `modulators` / `crossfadeMembers` / `silentReferences` are fine; `silentVoices` / `leaks` / `dead` are defects. Gain is only read from params that were set once (`ParamStub.automated`), because an envelope's last automation target is `0.0001` for every voice. **Result: 22/22 live, 0 leaks, 0 lifecycle throws.** The ten "silent clocks" (pluck, flute, organ, strings, bell, reese, acid, brass, clav, drumsynth — a 440 Hz oscillator at gain 0 on `ctx.destination` whose only job is to fire `onended` in an offline render, where `setTimeout` cannot) are pinned as infrastructure, and the wavetable's two permanently-muted frame sources are resolved as crossfade members by their live sibling, not an allowlist. Locked by `tests/instrument-graph-reachability.test.ts` (7 cases + a full parameter sweep, three overlapping notes, `noteOff`/`panic`/`dispose` per runtime) — **falsified** by deleting `amp.connect(output)` from the Clavinet again, which fails 3/7 with `clav NO LIVE SOURCE DEAD=[oscillator#3, oscillator#4, oscillator#5, oscillator#7, oscillator#14, oscillator#16, bufferSource#18]`.
- **A voice released during its attack was muted, not released (fixed 2026-10-24)** — the plugin audit reported **Spectral Pad at -68.5 dBFS** and **Texture Synth at -84.0 dBFS** as broken instruments (the question the Clavinet note above left open). Both were REAL defects: the voice-release path cancelled the envelope's pending automation with `cancelScheduledValues()`, which drops an in-flight ramp to the last `setValueAtTime()` — the envelope floor. A note released while its attack was still running therefore snapped to silence instead of fading out, and the release tail never sounded at all. The release path now cancels with HOLD semantics (`cancelAndHoldAtTime`, new `holdParamAt` in `src/instruments/envelope.ts`) across all 27 voice-release sites (`registry.ts` 24, `tsarNode.ts`, `choralFallback.ts`, `kopaNode.ts`); the panic/silence paths keep the plain cancel on purpose — a panic must cut, not fade. Measured: Spectral Pad -68.5 → **-16.5 dBFS**, Texture Synth -84.0 → **-17.0 dBFS**, both now `defaultAudible: true`; the two healthy instruments in the same run (choral -6.9, sampler -15.9) come out **bit-identical**, so the change has zero blast radius on instruments whose attack completes before the note-off. The audit's audition fixture was the second half: a fixed 0.35 s tap cannot audition a 0.6 s attack (the render then measures the attack slope, not the operating level), so `src/plugin-audit-audition.ts` now holds the note until the default ATTACK completes — identical for the 23 instruments whose attack is shorter than the tap. Locked by `tests/voice-envelope-release.test.ts` (12 specs: the hold semantics, the structural shape of every release path, the fixture contract — **5/5 mutations falsified**) and by the real-browser check `Spectral Pad: release during the attack fades from the attack level` in `src/browser-checks.ts`.
- **The plugin audit called a working pitch corrector broken (fixed 2026-10-25)** — the audit reported **Pitch Correct as "not processing in the host graph"** (`hostDelta` 4.9e-9, `hostMixDelta` 1.6e-8) while the real-browser check "amount 1 corrects toward the scale target" PASSED. Triage (measured, not assumed) found **no product defect**: the worklet does load in the export context, and on tonal material the effect changes the sound by 0.31. The audit was wrong twice, for two independent reasons. **(1) The fingerprint was a passthrough.** The host probe turns the knob with the biggest change against the DEFAULT render — and for a parameter whose default sits ON an extreme, that state is the one that switches the device OFF: `pitchCorrect.amount` defaults to 1 (full retune) and its minimum is 0 (NO retune), so the "strongest" extreme meant "no retune at all" (measured `dryDelta` = **0** — the effect does nothing there). The probe then compared the dry signal with the dry signal. **(2) The material could not excite the effect.** Every effect was fed the house template's drum bus, and a pitch corrector has nothing to correct in a kick: measured on the house doc with the kit's REAL drum samples (an empty sample bank renders the drum bus silent — a different failure, and one that must not be confused with this one), the drum bus moved the effect by **8.5e-5**, the 808 bass by **5.4e-9**, and the melodic Chords track by **0.108**; the old fingerprint on that same track read **6.9e-9**. The fix: the sweep now measures every extreme against the DRY render of the same stimulus (`dryDelta`), and the fingerprint rule (`src/plugin-audit-fingerprint.ts`, Node-pure so it can be proven in a unit test) refuses a state whose `dryDelta` is zero; the host probe measures the effect at its defaults AND in the fingerprint state and keeps the more audible one, and when the drum bus shows no processing at all it re-measures the effect on the doc's melodic tracks. The report now carries `hostMaterial` and `hostState`, because a verdict without its material is unreadable. Measured on the house doc (the committed baseline): `hostDelta` **4.9e-9 → 0.254**, `hostMixDelta` **1.6e-8 → 0.254**, `automationDelta` **4.0e-8 → 0.019**, material **Chords (melodic)**, state **effect at defaults**; `bypass == removed` and the JSON round-trip still render bit-comparable. The VERDICT is stable across three runs (host delta 0.125 / 0.254 / 0.260) but the absolute host numbers are **not bit-reproducible**: the sweep columns (`defaultPeak`, `bypassDelta`, every per-parameter delta and dry delta) come out identical to the last digit in every run, so the drift is in the project render's metrics, not in the measurement code — the mechanism is not pinned down and the house doc's drum material (a network-loaded curated layer) is the prime suspect. An audit host delta should be read as a VERDICT, not as a value. Locked by `tests/plugin-audit-host-probe.test.ts` (8 specs: the fingerprint rule proven on the real pitchCorrect sweep, the OLD rule shown to land on the passthrough, one mutation per structural contract — **6/6 detected in-test**, and the behavioural specs were falsified against a doctored copy of the rule) and by `tests/plugin-audit-measurement.test.ts` (the host verdict stays the OR of the timbre and the wiring probe — now pinned where the decision happens). Locked by `tests/voice-envelope-release.test.ts` (12 specs: the hold semantics, the structural shape of every release path, the fixture contract — **5/5 mutations falsified**) and by the real-browser check `Spectral Pad: release during the attack fades from the attack level` in `src/browser-checks.ts`.
- **The loudness-map drift gate measured a different kick than the map (fixed 2026-10-21)** — the browser check "presets: auditions finite/unclipped + loudness map in sync" reported KOPA drift and advised "run npm run presets:loudness" (e.g. `factory.kopa.techno.punch` measures −21.5 LUFS, map recorded −30.3). **The map was never stale** — the advice was wrong, and following it would have baked a sound the product never exports into the map. MEASURED on all 16 KOPA presets, through both syntheses: the map matches the **worklet** to **0.04 LU**, while the **native fallback** reads **6.2-12.5 LU LOUDER**. KOPA's event-queue worklet is also its production offline renderer (ADR 0035), so the map is recorded through the worklet (deliberate exception in `scripts/measure-preset-loudness.mjs`) — but the drift gate created a fresh `OfflineAudioContext` per preset and never loaded the worklet into it, so it measured the fallback and compared two different syntheses. Same failure class as the two browser checks fixed on 2026-10-24: a check that measures a path the product does not ship is not a gate. The fix: the gate now arms the production path (loads the worklet, refuses to measure the fallback if it is missing, and calls `prepareOfflineRender()` so the queued note events are seeded — Chromium does not pump a worklet's message queue during an offline render), and the list of worklet-path instruments has **one owner** (`src/presets/loudnessPath.ts`) read by both the map generator and the gate, so they cannot drift apart. **The map was NOT regenerated** — it was already correct. KOPA check: `[PASS] passed=16/16`. _(preset loudness 2026-10-21)_
- **The plugin audit called a working stereo imager broken (fixed 2026-10-21)** — the audit reported **SPAN as "not processing in the host graph"** (`hostDelta` 7.9e-8) and it was wrong for a **third, separate** reason: the house template is a **MONO** mix. MEASURED on the house doc with the kit's real drum samples: the drum bus renders `side = 0` bit-exactly (every source in it is a mono sample played down the middle) and so does every melodic track. SPAN is a per-band mid/side rescaler (`L' = M + w·S`, `R' = M − w·S`), so with `S = 0` in every band every width is a mathematical no-op — the processor's own header calls width 1 a "mathematically neutral pass-through". The audit compared a mono bus with a mono bus. The fix: the host probe has a **third material**, the same drum bus with its **pads** panned (a pad's pan is PRE-insert — a track's pan is post-insert and would change nothing), and it is tried **LAST**, so the 56 effects that already process keep exactly the measurement they had. MEASURED after the fix: `hostDelta` 7.9e-8 → **0.637**, `hostProcesses` true, material "Drums (drum bus, spread kit)", state "fingerprint midWidth=0, lowWidth=2" — the report names both, so the verdict is readable. The material rule moved to `src/plugin-audit-materials.ts` (Node-pure, so the rule that decides WHAT is measured is unit-testable without a browser — the same reason the fingerprint rule lives where it does) and joined the harness fingerprint, so a report measured before the change can no longer pass as current. Full audit: 57 effects, 25 instruments, **0 not processing** (vocoder + sidechain exempt by design), 86 dead parameters, GATE PASSED. _(plugin audit 2026-10-21)_
- **The audit's absolute numbers moved between runs because its DOCUMENT was random (fixed 2026-10-21)** — the plugin audit's VERDICT was stable but its host numbers wobbled: between two full audits **52 of 57 effects changed their `hostDelta`** (pitchCorrect 0.254 → 0.144, apeks 5.658 → 6.638) while **51 of 57 kept their sweep columns**. The split is the clue: the sweep renders a synthetic buffer, the host probe renders the **HOUSE PROJECT**. MEASURED, in 8 steps and two hypotheses killed by the data: the curated layer is constant (129 loaded / 0 failed / 17 skipped), the render consumes no ids, and two renders of the same doc in the same page differ by ONE float32 ULP — the format's quantum, not a variability source. The variance was in the _document_. The house template mints a fresh **random** id (`uid()` → `crypto.randomUUID()`) for every track, pattern and NOTE, and the render's ordering depends on those ids. Two templates with random ids render with an **rms spread of 7.1e-6** and a peak spread of **1.2e-4**; two with deterministic ids render **BIT-IDENTICALLY** (spread exactly 0). The audit's host delta is a **relative** metric — the difference of two nearly identical renders — so a 1e-6 difference becomes percent-level movement. That is why no threshold could fix it: the source of the variability had to go, not be bounded. The fix is in the **measurer, not the product**: the audit's document and every measurement unit now mint ids deterministically (`src/shared/ids.ts` already had `useDeterministicIds()`/`resetDeterministicIds()`, test-only until now), the document on base 0 and each unit on base 1 000 000 — so a unit's numbers do not depend on how many units ran before it (a resumed audit measures the same thing as a full one) and the two id spaces cannot collide. **MEASURED after the fix, two separate processes: eq `hostDelta` 1.1931903621546618 vs 1.1931903623248852 (1.4e-10 relative), pitchCorrect 0.13447833972998513 vs 0.13447834525076438 (4.1e-8), sirka 0.637010431134183 vs 0.6370104310058765 (2.0e-10) — against 25 % and 0.03 % before.** What remains is the **format's quantum, not the measurement**: two renders of the same document in the same process differ by **2.384185791015625e-7** — one float32 ULP at the signal's peak — in 26 250 of 111 815 samples, so the residual sits at the representation limit of the audio pipeline, and no report rounding is needed to hide it (the gate judges health, it does not diff numbers). **Locked by** `tests/plugin-audit-determinism.test.ts` (4 specs: two house templates built with deterministic ids carry identical ids, with the negative control that without the mode they differ; plus five structural contracts on the audit's own wiring — the document's base, each unit's base, the two bases ≥ 1000 apart, the scope restored in a `finally`, and every unit running its work INSIDE the scope — each with a mutation that has to break it). _(plugin audit 2026-10-21)_
- **The audit's SWEEP columns have a 5 % noise floor — the envelope term divides by a near-silent segment (measured 2026-10-21, NOT fixed)** — while the HOST columns are now reproducible (see the bullet above), the factory sweep still wobbles for a handful of parameters, and the mechanism is measured. `deltaVs` takes the **maximum** of six terms (rms, crest, side, centroid, dyn, envelope); the envelope term compares 16 per-segment RMS levels and divides each difference by **that segment's own level**, so a segment that is 60 dB below the signal turns a one-float32-ULP render difference into a **5 % relative difference**. MEASURED on `msEq`: all four frequency parameters (`midLowFreq`, `midHighFreq`, `sideLowFreq`, `sideHighFreq`) report **exactly the same constant 0.05036200376016288** — the signature of a metric floor, not of a per-parameter measurement — and that value sits **above the `RESPONSIVE_EPS` = 0.02 threshold**, so a parameter whose TRUE response is ~0 flips between DEAD and RESPONSIVE depending on one-ULP render jitter. Three `auditOneEffect("msEq")` calls **in the same page** already disagree: `sideHighFreq` read 0 on the first call and 0.05036200376016288 on the next two. Across runs the same three values recur (0, 0.004756, 0.050362) on different parameters — the earlier reading that the sweep columns were stable in 51 of 57 effects was true only because most parameters are far from the floor. **Not fixed here on purpose**: the fix is to ignore segments that are inaudible RELATIVE to the signal (not merely below 1e-9 absolute), and that changes what a delta MEANS — the harness fingerprint, the dead-parameter count in this document, and the work list other agents read from the report. It needs its own baseline round, not a drive-by edit. The host columns were left untouched because their values are far from the floor.
- **Two browser checks measured the offline worklet path without arming it (fixed 2026-10-24)** — `offline export parity: wavetable + granular` and `granular voice worklet` measured **0.0000** (silence) and failed, but nothing was broken: the offline worklet path creates its `AudioWorkletNode` inside `prepareOfflineRender()` and seeds the queued note events through `processorOptions`, because Chromium does not pump a worklet's message queue during an `OfflineAudioContext` render. The export renderer arms exactly that call (`renderer.ts` → `AudioEngine.prepareOfflineRender`), the two checks did not, so the node never existed. Measured A/B on the same render: granular worklet **0.0000 → 0.3270**, wavetable offline **0.0000 → 0.3449**, granular offline **0.0000 → 0.3244**. A check that fails for a non-defect reason is not a gate — it only hides the failures that matter.
- **The browser gate could not reach its checks (fixed 2026-10-24)** — `npm run test:browser` died on `page.goto` before a single check ran, in three different ways: the navigation had a **120 s** budget while the app page needs Vite to transform the whole module graph before `domcontentloaded` can fire (measured **198.4 s** on an idle box, over 300 s while other agent sessions were committing into the same worktree); the dev server had HMR and a file watcher on, so a concurrent session's save became a Vite full page reload that destroyed the execution context the checks run in (the retry re-ran the whole 20-minute suite and still lost the race 3× in a row); and the export-download check left an **unhandled promise rejection** pending — `waitForEvent("download")` arms its own timeout, so if the click in between threw, the later rejection had no handler and Node killed the runner before it could print a summary. The runner now boots with `BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS = 600_000` and `hmr: false, watch: null` (the same way the plugin-audit runner already did) and keeps the download error instead of dropping it. The gate is **425 checks**; what it still reports is the pre-existing set (KOPA preset loudness map, the live↔offline fx null-test group, RR variant derivation, PDC export alignment) plus the perf budgets, which flip with machine load — the same suite measured `house` at **0.37×** (pass) and **1.60×** (fail) minutes apart on the same code, so a red perf line here is a load reading, not a regression.
- **The 2026-09-27 report's mod-matrix "dead params" are 55 measurement artifacts** — `docs/plugin-audit-2026-09-27.report.json` flags 69 mod-matrix params as inert (11 instruments × `modASrc`/`modADst`/`modBSrc`/`modBDst`/`modLfoRate`, plus `modAAmt` ×11 and `modBAmt` ×3). `src/plugin-audit-checks.ts:764` sweeps one param at a time with siblings at defaults, but `scheduleVoiceModMatrix` allocates its source buses only when a slot has a non-zero **amount** — both default to 0, so the graph is never built and the sweep provably cannot move a sample. Proven structurally by `tests/mod-matrix-dead-param-artifact.test.ts` (4, no audio needed): an all-defaults voice allocates exactly 1 node; sweeping those three params to any value, directly or via `setParameter`, allocates 0 more; a non-zero `modAAmt` **does** build the graph. **55 of 69 are false findings.** The remaining 14 are **unconfirmed** — `modDstOptions(false)` returns `{value: 0, label: "OFF"}`, so the default `modADst: 0` is a deliberately inert destination on 21 of 22 instruments, but that does not account for all 11 flagged `modAAmt`. Recommended fix: make the sweep group-aware (force a live destination when sweeping an amount) so the next report stops re-reporting them. The next unexplained cluster is **`glide`, inert on 7 instruments** — not mod-matrix related, and the first thing to chase.

## Verification baseline — 2026-10-03, commit `f2456243` + `aff1cd37`

Full-suite run against a CLEAN worktree at HEAD (not the shared dirty tree — see `docs/MULTI-AGENT-GUARDRAILS.md` §6), plus targeted re-runs of every failing family after repairs. This is the first honest whole-repo measurement since 2026-09-14.

| Gate                                                                   | Result at `f2456243`+`aff1cd37`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Full Vitest suite (740 files / 8094 tests, 2037 s)                     | **≈7,991 pass / 37 fail / 139 skip** — every failure classified below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Playwright E2E — idea → export (`tests/e2e/16-idea-to-export.spec.ts`) | **chromium 1/1 passed**; webkit skipped by design — Windows WebKit ships no media stack, so an offline render cannot produce a WAV there (the same engine gap `playwright.config.ts` already documents for specs 01–08). Closes the last unproven seam: intent text → project model → transport → AudioEngine → offline renderer → WAV encoder → browser download. Spec 03 proved generation mutates the doc, spec 15 proved intent mutates the doc, `verify-browser.mjs` proved a JSON project downloads — nothing proved the last mile. Asserts on REAL samples, not on bytes: a minimal RIFF reader (`readWavFacts`) walks `fmt `/`data` and measures peak + RMS, because a silent or truncated WAV is still a "successful" download. Measured on the House template: 1 147 302 bytes, 44 100 Hz stereo 16-bit, peak 0.891, RMS 0.220. Those measurements are written to `master-wav-facts.json` BEFORE the level assertions on purpose — a report emitted after a failing expect never runs, and a level regression is exactly the case where the numbers matter. |
| `tsc --noEmit` on committed sources                                    | clean (committed state; the shared tree's 6 errors are a concurrent wave's uncommitted WIP)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Bundle budgets (`check-bundle-size`)                                   | OK — DAW 4303/5000 KB, entry 257/1070, **studio boot path 1864/2400 KB** (F2 anchor diet 2026-10-04: intent engine + dice heavy set + EffectRack + curated out of the static boot closure, −273 KB), shipped total 5823 KB                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `format:check`                                                         | authored files clean; repo-wide deviations documented (`docs/FORMAT-CHECK-DEVIATIONS.md`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

**Repairs this measurement surfaced (all fixed in the same session):**

- **HEAD did not build on a fresh checkout.** `.gitignore` line `listening/` (unanchored, meant for the root-level golden-review output) silently swallowed the real `src/listening/` source dir, so `5cb9713d` shipped `blind-ab.ts` + its test importing a module git never tracked → all 22 mcp-* test files failed collection (`Failed to resolve import ../listening/abx-stats`). Fixed in `f2456243` (module landed, all four listening rules root-anchored) → the whole family re-ran **283/283**.
- **Two standing invariant drifts closed** in `aef3e62c`: scheduler driver's `ctx.createGain()` moved behind an engine-managed `createContextGain()` factory seam (invariant #7, new degrade-path test), and the audio-engine `as any` budget pin follows its real refactor down (17→16).
- **Stale test fixture** for `setProject` re-entrancy caught up with the engine decomposition (metering/warpManager collaborator stubs) in `aff1cd37` — 3/3.

**The 37 remaining failures — every one classified, none unowned:**

| Cluster                                                                                                                                                                                         | Tests | Classification                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Grooves calibration (drone 5, boombap 2, postrock 2, three-family-wave, metric-accent, drum-rr-declick feel map)                                                                                | ~12   | Stale count/parser pins behind an in-flight grooves wave (`b7a119c0` is mid-campaign) — owned by the grooves session; 5 of them already pass with that session's uncommitted working-tree files  |
| Intent-model + training pins (ai-baseline frozen hashes, intent-model-artifact/loader/sft-prompt, symbolic-melodic 2, symbolic-prior 4, melodic-embedding, creative-task-contract, model-packs) | ~14   | Model-artifact pins vs regenerated local artifacts (the manifest is actively re-pinned in the shared tree); some depend on gitignored local-only datasets — owned by the intent/training session |
| Engine source-grep pins (trigger-engine, warp-manager, fx-tempo-sync)                                                                                                                           | 3     | Stale verbatim-marker pins after code moved in the decomposition — already green with the concurrent session's uncommitted files; they commit their fix                                          |
| Vendor SDK gates (asio-host, clap-host)                                                                                                                                                         | 2     | Environment-dependent by design (SDK bytes are deliberately gitignored) — documented owner gates                                                                                                 |
| `setProject` re-entrancy                                                                                                                                                                        | 3     | FIXED in `aff1cd37`                                                                                                                                                                              |

**Not verified in this pass:** real-browser verifier, factory-preset audio QA, 300 s soaks — they need a live browser host and remain owner-gated (see below).

## What this document is NOT

- It is **not** a roadmap. See `RELEASE_ROADMAP.md`, `EDIT-ROADMAP.md`, `INSTRUMENT-ROADMAP.md`, `DSP-ROADMAP.md`, `SCENE-MODE-ROADMAP.md`, `INTENT_ENGINE.md` and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION-ROADMAP-MRT2-GENERATIVE-TRACKS.md` for planned work.
- It is **not** an architecture reference. See `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/adr/`.
- It is **not** a user-facing feature description. See `README.md`.
- It is **not** a changelog. See `git log --oneline` and the commit messages; the "Recent additions" section above is a curated digest, not the full history.

When you change a number above (e.g. you add a new instrument and `INSTRUMENT_DEFS` grows to 16), update this file in the same commit. The whole point is that there's exactly one place readers look for "what ships today".
